The reporter expects: changing `defaults.model` changes everyone (that is what\nthe anchor is for); changing `coder.settings.model` is refused, and they can explicitly ask for coder's copy to be\nexpanded so they can override it.\n\n## Current State\n- On `main` today the reported file cannot be used at all. `get` and `set` both exit 4 with\n  `…/troupe.yml: defaults: anchor '&d' is not supported (anchors, aliases and merge keys are refused)`. But no theatre that uses anchors can be read or\n  changed either.\n- config-foundation (`config/loader.py`): `_refuse_properties` refuses any `&anchor` on a node. A defined alias\n  is caught through its anchor, and an undefined alias is a composer error. A plain `<<` key is refused in the\n  mapping builder. The model (`config/model.py`) has as an invariant that \"no node has an explicit tag or anchor\".\n  It has no notion of a node reached through an alias.\n- config-set-writeback (`config/writeback/`): `resolve_target` → `ReplaceTarget`/`AddKeyTarget`/`AppendTarget`\n  use each node's `origin` (file + span) as the physical place to patch. assistant: I've finished the discovery for issue #2 and committed it on `main` as `2627541` (not pushed). Nothing in the code has changed yet, so the alias behaviour itself still has to be built.\n\n**What the issue really is.** I ran the reporter's file against current `main`. Both `set coder.settings.model sonnet` and `get` exit 4 with "anchor '&d' is not supported", and the file is left untouched. So the corruption they describe can't happen here, because anchors are still refused (decision H, which held them back until step 6). The issue is really the step 6 "Aliases" contract: files with anchors should load, and `set` should follow the new rules.\n\n**Path:** one new spec, `config-aliases`, plus short step-6 notes in the `config-foundation` and `config-set-writeback` specs. Your instructions said to proceed, so I chose this path myself instead of stopping to ask. It's easy to change if you'd rather go another way.\n\n**Approach:** this follows the same pattern as step 5.\n- When loading, every alias is replaced by the content of its anchor, and each node reached that way remembers which alias it came through.\n- That means `get`, `show`, `history`, reconcile and `watch` need no changes.\n- Before writing, `set` checks the path. If it passes through an alias, it refuses with exit 3 and mentions `--expand-alias`. A path that only touches the anchor is written at the anchor, so every alias sees the change.\n- `--expand-alias` first swaps the alias for a block-style copy with the `# expanded from *<name> by troupe` comment, then does the normal `set` on the copy. Both changes are written together and checked once.\n\n**Open choices for the requirements step.** The brief lists 12 with cautious defaults; these are the ones most worth your attention:\n1. **Merge keys (`<<`):** still exit 4 when loading. The contract only says they're never written.\n2. **Setting the alias's own key** (for example `set coder.settings {…}`): also exit 3 unless `--expand-alias` is given.\n3. **Key line that already has a comment:** expansion is refused with exit 3, rather than replacing or editing the user's comment.\n4. **Alias inside a flow collection:** expansion is refused with exit 3.\n5. **Several aliases on one path:** each is expanded, outermost first, and the copies don't repeat `&name`.\n\n**Files:**\n- `spec/spec/config-aliases/brief.md` (new)\n- `spec/steering/roadmap.md` (new "Step 6" section and spec list entry)\n\n**Next step:** run `/method-command config-aliases`, or `/method-command config-aliases` to go straight through requirements, design and tasks. The issue is fixed only once `/method-command config-aliases` has been run and committed. Afterwards, `config-foundation` and `config-set-writeback` need their notes added with `/method-command`.

Broken forms still exit 4: an undefined alias, an alias inside its own anchor, or an anchor on a key.\n- **Setting at an anchor (4):** the change is made at the anchor and shows up at every alias, with history events for every affected path. The anchor stays when its value is replaced.\n- **Refusal (5):** a path reached through an alias, including the alias's own key, exits 3. stderr names the file, PATH and `*name`, and mentions `--expand-alias`.\n- **`--expand-alias` (6–8):** it takes no value and combines with `--reason` and `--in-include`. It writes a block-style copy with `# expanded from *<name> by troupe`, then sets the value in the copy. Nested aliases are expanded outermost first. It refuses with exit 3 when the alias is inside a flow collection or the key's line already has a comment.\n- **Merge keys and safety (9–10):** troupe never writes `<<`, so any write through a merge key exits 3. Every write is checked by reading it back and is written atomically.\n\n**One change from the brief:** merge keys now load, instead of staying exit 4. The contract names them, so files that use them are now covered, and reading them can't change a byte. Writes through them are still refused.\n\n## Document Status\n- `spec/spec/config-aliases/requirements.md` now includes the contract text from issue #2 and 17 choices with cautious defaults for you to review.\n- `spec.json` is at phase `requirements-generated`.\n- `roadmap.md` now has the corrected merge-key note.\n- Committed on `main` as `7e9eb73`.\n\n## Review Gate\nPassed in two passes. It also fixed two sentences that weren't in EARS form.\n\n## Next Steps\nThe decisions most worth your attention are choices 1, 2, 5, 6, 9 and 17. Once you approve:\n```\n/method-command config-aliases\n```\nUse `-y` to approve the requirements and go straight to design. `/method-command config-aliases` is optional.

IncludeDirective?) and compares to reloaded tree, how the indentation step ...[truncated 1034 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; uv run python - <<'EOF'\nimport ruamel.yaml\nfrom ruamel.yaml.events import AliasEvent\nsrc=\"\"\"defaults: &d\n  model: opus\ncoder:\n  settings: *d   # c\n  more: {a: *d}\nm:\n  <<: [*d, {x: 1}]\n  own: 2\ns: &n foo\nt: *n\n\"\"\"\ny=ruamel.yaml.YAML()\nroot=y.compose(src)\nprint(type(root).__name__)\nfor k,v in root.value:\n    print(k.value, type(v).__name__, id(v), v.anchor, v.start_mark.index, v.end_mark.index)\nd=root.value[0][1]; c=root.value[1][1].value[0][1]\nprint(\"same object:\", d is c)\nprint(\"merge in m:\", [(k.value, type(v).__name__) for k,v in root.value[2][1].value], getattr(root.value[2][1],'merge',None))\nfor e in y.parse(src):\n    if isinstance(e, AliasEvent): print(\"alias\", e.anchor, e.start_mark.index, e.end_mark.index, repr(src[e.start_mark.index:e.end_mark.index]))\nprint(ruamel.yaml.__version__)\n# recursion\ntry:\n    y.compose(\"a: &a\\n  b: *a\\n\"); print(\"recursive composed ok\")\nexcept Exception as ex: print(\"recursive:\", type(ex).__name__, ex)\nimport warnings\nwith warnings.catch_warnings(record=True) as w:\n    warnings.simplefilter(\"always\")\n    try: r=y.compose(\"a: &a 1\\nb: &a 2\\nc: *a\\n\"); print(\"dup anchor ok\", r.value[2][1].value, [str(x.message)[:80] for x in w])\n    except Exception as ex: print(\"dup:\", type(ex).__name__, str(ex)[:120])\ntry: y.compose(\"&k a: 1\\n\"); print(\"anchor key ok\")\nexcept Exception as ex: print(type(ex).__name__)\nEOF"}

assistant: Below is the factual summary you asked for. I ran one read-only check against ruamel.yaml 0.19.1 (installed in `.venv`): composing `a: &x {b: 1}\nc: *x\nd:\n  <<: *x\n  e: 2\n` gives `a is c == True`, `anchor == 'x'`, and marks 3..12 (the anchor's span). The `<<` key node has tag `'tag:yaml.org,2002:merge'`, `style None`, `value '<<'`. Its mapping's `.merge` is `None` at compose time and it still has 2 entries.\n\n## 1. `src/troupe/config/model.py`\nAll dataclasses are `@dataclass(frozen=True)` and all collections are tuples. The docstring invariant (l.51) says no node has an explicit tag or anchor.\n- `LineEnding(Enum)` l.63: `NONE`, `LF`, `CRLF`, `MIXED`.\n- `SourceFile` l.73: `path: Path` (as opened, used in messages), `real_path: Path` (`resolve()`, identity), `text: str`, `line_ending: LineEnding`.\n- `Span` l.83: `start: int`, `end: int`. These are character indices; `end` is exclusive.\n- `Origin` l.91: `file: SourceFile`, `span: Span`.\n- `IncludeKind(Enum)` l.98: `NONE`, `YAML="yaml-include"`, `TEXT="text-include"`.\n- `IncludeRef` l.107: `kind`, `spec: str`, `target: SourceFile`, `directive: Origin`, `form: ConfigMapping|None=None` (the `$ref` mapping as written), `inner: IncludeRef|None=None` (chained include).\n- `ScalarStyle` l.125: `PLAIN`, `SINGLE_QUOTED`, `DOUBLE_QUOTED`, `LITERAL`, `FOLDED`. `CollectionStyle` l.135: `BLOCK`, `FLOW`.\n- `ConfigScalar` l.143: `value: ScalarValue`, `style: ScalarStyle|None` (None only for text includes), `origin`, `include=None`.\n- `MappingEntry` l.153: `key: str`, `key_origin: Origin`, `key_style: ScalarStyle`, `value: ConfigNode`.\n- `MergeLayers` l.163: `base: ConfigMapping`, `over: ConfigMapping`.\n- `ConfigMapping` l.174: `entries: tuple[MappingEntry,...]`, `style`, `origin`, `include=None`, `layers: MergeLayers|None=None`, plus `get(key) -> MappingEntry|None`.\n- `ConfigSequence` l.192: `items`, `style`, `origin`, `include=None`.\n- `ConfigNode = ConfigScalar | ConfigMapping | ConfigSequence` (runtime union, l.202).\n- `ConfigDocument` l.206: `root_file`, `root: ConfigMapping`, `files: tuple[SourceFile,...]`, plus `include_kind(node)`.\n\n**Node identity:** nodes are not shared for diamonds. Only the `SourceFile` is shared. `_Builder.include` (loader l.396) calls `_compose(target)` and builds new nodes for every place. `shared.py`'s docstring confirms this ("loaded once per place, as separate node objects"), which is why written locations are keyed by `(real_path, start, end)`. Some code does key on object identity:\n- `FileLayout._dash_cache` uses `id(sequence)` (layout l.449/467).\n- `target._text_include_targets` uses `id(plain directive)` (l.596-622).\n\n## 2. `src/troupe/config/loader.py`\n- Entry points:\n  - `load_config(config_file: Path, overrides: Mapping[Path,str]|None=None) -> ConfigDocument` (l.90).\n  - `load_include(including: SourceFile, spec: str, overrides) -> ConfigNode` (l.117), used by `shared._Loading`.\n- `_Loader` (l.134): reads files and caches by real path. Methods: `read_source`, `read_include(target, including, key_path)`, `_remember`. It keeps a `files` list.\n- `_compose(source) -> Node|None` (l.226) calls `YAML(typ="rt").compose(source.text)`. That is ruamel's round-trip composer. The loader works on composed **Nodes**, never on events. Errors go through `_describe_marked_error` (l.242).\n- `_Builder(source, loader, chain)` (l.252):\n  - `build(node, path: ConfigPath) -> ConfigNode` (l.264) handles scalars and sequences inline. A scalar starting with `file:` goes to `self.include(...)`.\n  - `_scalar` (l.284), `_build_mapping(node, path)` (l.291), `_ref_value` (l.348).\n  - `include(directive, spec, path, *, form=None)` (l.361), `_refuse_cycle` (l.427).\n  - `_refuse_properties(node, path)` (l.437), `_own_property_text` (l.456), `_scalar_style` (l.471), `_origin(node)` (l.479).\n  - There are no functions named `_build_sequence` or `_build_scalar`.\n- **Spans:** `_origin` returns `Origin(self._source, Span(int(node.start_mark.index), int(node.end_mark.index)))`.\n- **Refusals:**\n  - `_refuse_properties` l.439: `if node.anchor is not None` raises `ConfigLoadError(f"anchor '&{node.anchor}' is not supported (anchors, aliases and merge keys are refused)", file=..., path=_text(path))`.\n  - Tags are refused from `node.ctag.handle` or a source prefix starting with `!`.\n  - It is called on every value in `build`, on keys (l.308) and on `$ref` values (l.350).\n  - `_MERGE_KEY = "<<"` (l.86). It is refused in `_build_mapping` l.309 when `key_style is PLAIN and key == "<<"`, with "merge key '<<' is not supported (...)" and `path` = key path. The quoted form `"<<"` is an ordinary key.\n  - Per the docstring (l.16-17), a defined alias is caught by its anchor, which comes first in the document. An undefined alias is a composer error, reported without a path.\n- **Key paths:** `ConfigPath` is threaded through recursion with `path.child(key)` / `path.child(index)`. `_text(path)` gives `None` at the root.\n- **Includes:** `include()` treats a non-YAML suffix as a TEXT include. The result is `ConfigScalar(target.text, style=None, Origin(target, Span(0,len)))`. A YAML include goes through `_refuse_cycle`, `read_include` and `_compose`, then a new `_Builder(target, loader, chain+target).build(composed, path)`. The outcomes are:\n  - With overrides: `deep_merge(embedded, form, include=ref)` (overrides.py l.41). This produces `layers=MergeLayers(base, over)`. Scalars and lists are the nodes of their own layer.\n  - Empty target: a null scalar at `Span(0,0)` with `include=ref`.\n  - Otherwise: `dataclasses.replace(embedded, include=replace(ref, inner=embedded.include))`.\n- **`$ref` form:** `_build_mapping` builds the entries. For the `is_ref_key` entry, the value stays as written via `_ref_value`, then `self.include(directive, spec, path, form=mapping)`.\n- **Telling an alias apart:** today the loader cannot. An alias returns the same ruamel `Node` object, with the anchor's `start_mark`/`end_mark`, and `build` would just recurse into it again. No visited set or `id()` tracking exists in `_Builder`. The only way to tell an alias occurrence apart would be to track `id(node)` of nodes already visited (the first visit is the anchor, later ones are aliases). The alias's own text position (`*x`) is not on the node.\n\n## 3. `src/troupe/config/writeback/`\nThe import chain (`__init__.py`) is value → compare → layout → edits → scalar_render → block_scalar → collection_render → target → planner → verify → leaf_diff → atomic → setter. There is no `writeback/` module for `shared`; it is used by the planner.\n\n- **setter**: `set_value(theatre, path: ConfigPath, value: NewValue, context: ChangeContext, *, in_include=False) -> SetOutcome` (l.44). The flow is:\n  1. `resolve_target(document, path, value, in_include=)`.\n  2. A no-op returns when `plain_equal(current, intended, ordered=False)`.\n  3. `plan = plan_set(document, request)`.\n  4. `request = replace(request, intended=plan.intended)`.\n  5. `after = verify_patch(theatre.config_file, document, patches, request)`.\n  6. `diff_leaves(document, after)`.\n  7. `write_patches(patches, path)` (l.84).\n  8. `theatre.history.record_changes(changes, context)`; on failure it calls `_restore`.\n- **target**:\n  - `resolve_target(document, path, value, *, in_include=False) -> SetRequest` (l.163). It refuses `$ref` segments with `WriteRefusedError`. It calls `walk_path(document.root, path)` (resolve.py l.61, a walk over `entry.value`/`items` that returns `PathWalk(path, nodes, consumed, stop: WalkStop)`), then `_target(walk, in_include=)` (l.210). It computes `current = root_plain(document)` and `intended = splice(current, path, new_to_plain(value), text_content)`.\n  - `WriteTarget = ReplaceTarget | AddKeyTarget | AppendTarget` (l.129):\n    - `ReplaceTarget(node, parent: ConfigMapping|ConfigSequence, key: str|int)` (l.59).\n    - `AddKeyTarget(mapping, missing: tuple[str,...], placement: KeyPlacement)` (l.110).\n    - `AppendTarget(sequence)` (l.122).\n  - `KeyPlacement(mapping|None, convert: IncludeSite|None, prefix)` (l.95). `IncludeSite(node, parent, key, include=None)` with a `.ref` property (l.69).\n  - `SetRequest(path, target, value, current: PlainMapping, intended: PlainMapping, in_include=False)` (l.134).\n  - Other public functions: `place_new_keys` (l.279), `document_root` (l.404), `text_include_value` (l.152).\n- **planner**:\n  - `plan_set(document, request) -> Plan(patches: tuple[FilePatch,...], intended)` (l.186). `FilePatch(file: SourceFile, new_text: str)` (l.169).\n  - `_Planner` (l.218) holds `_layouts`, `_edits` (one list of `TextEdit` per `real_path`), `_whole` (whole-file text includes), `_removals`, `_additions` and `_conversions`. The last three are keyed by `WrittenLocation`. `changes = WrittenChanges(path=)`.\n  - Entry methods: `replace` (l.250; uses `physical_entry(parent, key)` for merged parents, then `layout.entry_slot`/`item_slot`), `add_key` (l.278), `append` (l.325).\n  - `change_slot(layout, node, slot, value, path, at, where)` (l.581) does, in order: `changes.claim`, an equality check, `_change_include` for include roots, then `_change_value` (l.619). `_change_value` covers `_replace_scalar` (l.1279), `_rewrite_block` (l.1171), `_merge*` (l.711-1029) and `_replace_value` (l.1197).\n  - `patches()` (l.1380) runs `apply_edits(layout, edits)` per file in `document.files` order. It refuses with "the change would not modify any file" when no file changes.\n  - `_add` refuses a file that would be changed in two ways. `_guard` turns a layout `ValueError` into a refusal.\n- **`_replace_value` (scalar → block collection):**\n  1. `extent = layout.inline_extent(slot)`. In a FLOW parent it uses `render_flow_value`.\n  2. Otherwise it reads `comment = layout.comment_start(line_of(extent.end))` and `body = layout.body_lines(slot)`.\n  3. It renders `render_block_value(value, slot.owner_column, slot.in_sequence, ctx, compact=comment is None, preferred=)`, which returns `(inline, following)`.\n  4. The inline part replaces the extent as an `EditKind.INLINE` edit. If the inline part is empty, the old inline text is deleted (from `indicator_end` when there is no comment).\n  5. The body lines are replaced by `following` as a `LINES` edit. With no body, `_following_edit` inserts at the start of the next line.\n  - `_context` builds `RenderContext(step=layout.indent_step(), dash_offset=layout.dash_offset(), line_break=layout.line_break_at(i), path)`.\n- **layout**: `FileLayout.build(file, document)` (l.106). `ValueSlot(file, path, parent_style, in_sequence, owner_column, indicator_end, node)` (l.49).\n  - **Indent step:** `_detect_indent_step` (l.683) uses the first nested BLOCK mapping (in document order of key position, via `_ordered` over `_block_entries_in_file`). The step is the column of its first key minus the parent key's column. The default is `DEFAULT_INDENT_STEP = 2` (l.33). `_detect_dash_offset` (l.697) works the same way for block lists and defaults to the step.\n  - **End-of-line comments:** `comment_start(line) -> int|None` (l.210) returns the first `#` after `_floors[line]` (from `_token_floors`, l.562: the greatest token end on the line) that starts the content or follows a space.\n  - **`inline_extent(slot) -> Span`** (l.285) works on `_local(slot.node)` (l.396: the `$ref` form, the node, or the directive span, and `ValueError` if the node is not written in this file). It returns:\n    - an empty null: `(indicator_end, indicator_end)`;\n    - a block scalar: the header;\n    - a scalar or flow collection: its span;\n    - a block collection on a later line: empty at `indicator_end`;\n    - a compact item: start to line end, minus the comment.\n  - Other methods: `body_lines(slot)` (l.314), `content_end` (l.272), `entry_slot` (l.232), `item_slot` (l.247), `insertion_point` (l.346), `entries_column` (l.334).\n  - Walks over written nodes use `_written`/`_children`/`written_parts`. `_ordered` notes that nodes visited more than once (diamonds) are handled by sorting.\n- **edits**: `EditKind.INLINE`/`LINES` (l.22), `TextEdit(start, end, text, kind)` (l.30), `apply_edits(layout, edits) -> str` (l.39). Overlapping edits are refused (`_check`). After INLINE edits, end-of-line comments are moved back to their columns (`_comments_to_fix`, `_align`).\n- **compare**: `IncludeDirective(text, content, kind=None, overrides=None)` (l.40); `kind` and `overrides` are excluded from equality. `PlainValue`/`PlainMapping` are type aliases. Functions: `node_to_plain(node)` (l.73), `root_plain(document)` (l.94), `new_to_plain(value)` (l.116), `plain_equal(a, b, *, ordered)` (l.149), `splice(root, path, value, text_content) -> PlainMapping` (l.187, implemented by `_Splicer`: merges mapping over mapping and list over list by index, keeps text-include directives, replaces YAML directives).\n- **shared** (choice 18): the "change appears at every place" logic.\n  - `WrittenLocation = (real_path, start, end)`, `written_location(origin)` (l.93).\n  - `WrittenChanges` (l.131) has fields `replaced`, `removed`, `added`, `converted`, `texts`, `files` and `claimed`. Methods: `claim/replace/remove/add/convert/text`. A differing value at the same location raises `CONFLICT` (exit 3).\n  - `with_other_places(document, intended, path, changes) -> PlainMapping` (l.262) works in three steps:\n    1. Every changed location must be in `_reached(root, path)` (layers along PATH plus the subtree at PATH); otherwise it refuses.\n    2. `before`/`after = rebuilt_plain(root, changes[, _Loading])` uses `_Rebuild` (l.457), which recomputes from written layers, applies changes by written location and re-merges `layers` with `_merge`.\n    3. `_graft` keeps `intended` along PATH and takes `after` off PATH wherever it differs from `before`.\n- **verify**: `verify_patch(config_file, document, patches, request) -> ConfigDocument` (l.26).\n  - Overrides are every `document.files` text plus the patched texts, then `load_config(config_file, overrides)`.\n  - A `ConfigLoadError` becomes `WriteRefusedError("written value cannot be read back: …")`.\n  - It compares `plain_equal(root_plain(after), request.intended, ordered=True)`. On a difference: "written configuration differs from the intended configuration" (also listing other patched files).\n  - The intended tree is `splice` from target, plus `with_other_places` from the planner. `IncludeDirective` is in the plain tree.\n- **leaf_diff**: `diff_leaves(before, after) -> tuple[LeafChange,...]` (l.9). It uses `flatten_leaves`, giving SetChange entries in `after` order, then RemoveChange.\n- **value**: `NewScalar(value, spelling)`, `NewMapping(entries)`, `NewSequence(items)`, `NewValue`. `parse_value(text, path) -> NewValue` (l.55) uses `_Parser` with the rt composer, accepts flow only, and raises `UsageError`.\n- **collection_render**:\n  - `RenderContext(step, dash_offset, line_break, path)`, `SiblingStyles(key, value)`.\n  - `render_block_entry(key|None, value, column, siblings, ctx) -> str` (l.60).\n  - `render_block_value(value, owner_column, in_sequence, ctx, *, compact=True, preferred=None) -> (inline, following)` (l.80). A nested mapping goes at `owner+step`, a list at `owner+dash_offset`, an item at `owner+2`.\n  - `render_flow_entry`, `render_ref_form_lines`, `render_flow_ref_form`, `render_flow_value(value, ctx)`.\n  - It renders `NewValue` only, not `ConfigNode`.\n- **scalar_render**: `render_scalar(value: NewScalar, context, preferred, path) -> str` (l.48) and `render_key(key, context, preferred, *, path=None)` (l.68). It re-exports `yaml_style` and refuses lone surrogates.\n- **block_scalar**: literal rendering and rewrite. Functions: `render_literal(value, indent, line_break) -> (header, content)` (l.87), `literal_fits`, `chomping_for`, `wrap_paragraph`, `rewrite_block(layout, slot, scalar, value) -> list[TextEdit]` (l.161, which keeps the indicator and indentation and rewrites folded blocks by paragraph).\n- **atomic**: `write_atomically(file: SourceFile, text: str)` (l.25) writes a temp file `.<name>.troupe-tmp-<hex>`, then fsync and rename to `real_path`. `restore_file(file)` (l.61). Failures raise `WriteRefusedError`.\n\n## 4. CLI and errors\n- `src/troupe/cli/set_command.py`:\n  - `_SetArgumentParser(TroupeArgumentParser)` (l.55) overrides `_parse_optional`: a single-dash argument other than `-h` is a positional.\n  - `add_set_subparser` (l.92) registers `path`, `value`, `--reason` (metavar TEXT, `default=None`, store) and `--in-include` (`store_true`), and sets `parser.__class__ = _SetArgumentParser`.\n  - `handle_set(args, out)` (l.68) runs `ConfigPath.parse`, `parse_value`, `open_theatre`, then `set_value(..., cli_context(args.actor, args.reason), in_include=args.in_include)`.\n- `cli/shell.py` l.19: `TroupeArgumentParser.error` raises `UsageError(f"{self.prog}: {message}")`.\n- `cli/main.py` `run()` l.105: `except TroupeError` writes `f"{error}\n"` to stderr and returns `int(error.exit_code)`.\n- `src/troupe/config/errors.py`:\n  - `ExitCode`: `OK=0`, `USAGE=2`, `WRITE_REFUSED=3`, `LOAD=4`, `PATH=5`.\n  - `format_message(*, file, path, problem)` returns `"<file>: <path>: <problem>"`, omitting whichever is unset.\n  - `TroupeError(problem, *, file: Path|None=None, path: str|None=None)`; `__str__` uses `format_message`.\n  - Subclasses: `UsageError`, `WriteRefusedError` (exit 3), `ConfigLoadError` (exit 4), `PathError` (exit 5), with `PathNotFoundError` and `WrongNodeKindError` under `PathError`.\n\n## 5. Anchor checks\n- `src/troupe/config/yaml_style.py`: `_MERGE_KEY = "<<"` (l.70). `_plain_possible` l.170 refuses a plain `<<` in key contexts. `_Pick: TypeAlias = Callable[[Node], Node | None]` (l.175). `_probes` (l.179). `_probe(document, pick, text)` (l.197) composes with rt and requires `ScalarNode`, `style is None`, `node.anchor is None` (l.209), `ctag.handle is None` and equal text.\n- `writeback/value.py` `_Parser._refuse_properties` l.144: `if node.anchor is not None: raise self._not_flow(f"anchor '&{node.anchor}' (anchors and aliases are refused)")`. This is a `UsageError` (exit 2), with the message "VALUE is not a single flow node: …".\n\n## 6. show emitter\n- `src/troupe/cli/yaml_output.py`: `render_yaml(node: ConfigNode, *, label: SourceLabeler | None) -> bytes` (l.84). It always starts at indent 0 and returns encoded bytes joined with `\n`.\n- The private helpers do accept an indent:\n  - `_collection_lines(node, indent) -> list[OutputLine]` (l.107)\n  - `_entry_lines(key, value, indent)` (l.120)\n  - `_item_lines(item, indent)` (l.130)\n  - `_scalar_lines(...)` (l.152)\n- It has a fixed `_INDENT = 2` and dash at +2, always uses LF, and normalises styles (no comments or original quoting). It lives in `troupe.cli`, and writeback must not import `troupe.cli` (per `writeback/__init__.py`).\n- Writeback's own block renderer is `collection_render.render_block_value`. It works on `NewValue`, not `ConfigNode`, but takes a file-specific step, dash offset and line break.\n\n## 7. Tests\n**Tests asserting the refusals:**\n- `tests/test_loader.py::test_refused_file_raises_a_load_error_naming_the_file_and_key_path`, parametrised from `REFUSALS` (l.465-474). The ids are `anchor-scalar`, `anchor-then-alias`, `anchor-on-mapping`, `anchor-on-key`, `anchor-on-list-item`, `anchor-on-root`, `anchor-after-tag`, `undefined-alias` (path None, "line 1"), `merge-key` (`<<`) and `nested-merge-key` (`a.<<`).\n- Also in `tests/test_loader.py`: `test_quoted_merge_key_text_is_an_ordinary_key` (l.505).\n- `tests/test_loader_includes.py::test_included_file_failing_single_file_checks_names_the_included_file`, with cases `"a: &x 1\n"` → `inc.a` and `"<<: {a: 1}\n"` → `inc.<<`.\n- `tests/test_e2e_locate_load.py::test_anchor_alias_or_merge_key_exits_4_naming_the_file_and_key_path` (l.252). It runs `config show a`.\n- `tests/test_e2e_includes.py::test_load_error_inside_included_file_names_the_included_file`, case `"x: &a 1\n"`.\n- `tests/test_set_cli.py::test_invalid_values_exit_2_naming_path_and_create_no_state`, param id `anchor` (`"&x 1"`).\n- `tests/writeback/test_value.py::test_values_that_are_not_a_single_flow_node_are_refused`, with cases `"&a x"`, `"[&a x, *a]"`, `"{&k a: 1}"`, `"*a"`, `"[*a]"`.\n- Merge-key quoting is covered in `tests/writeback/test_scalar_render.py` (l.161-164, l.412-416) and `tests/writeback/test_collection_render.py` l.303.\n\n**Helpers:**\n- `tests/support.py`:\n  - `CliResult(exit_code, stdout: bytes, stderr: str)`.\n  - Protocol `MakeTheatre(config=None, *, config_name="troupe.yml", files: TheatreFiles|None=None, name="theatre") -> Path`.\n  - Protocol `RunCli(*args, cwd=None) -> CliResult`.\n  - `write_theatre(root, config=None, *, config_name, files) -> Path`, `invoke_troupe(*args, cwd) -> CliResult` (runs the real console script), `troupe_executable()`.\n- `tests/conftest.py` fixtures: `make_theatre(tmp_path)` and `run_cli(tmp_path)`.\n- `tests/set_support.py`:\n  - `run_config(*args, cwd)` and `run_config_in_process(*args, cwd=None)` (calls `cli_main.run` in this process).\n  - `set_args(theatre, path, value)` returns `("set", path, value, "--theatre", str(theatre))`.\n  - `run_set(theatre, path, value) -> CliResult`, `assert_file_bytes(path, expected)`, `snapshot_tree(root) -> TreeSnapshot` (excludes `.troupe/history.sqlite*`).\n  - Test docstrings start with `Req X.Y: ...`.
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; uv run python - <<'EOF'\nimport inspect, ruamel.yaml\nfrom ruamel.yaml.composer import Composer\nfrom ruamel.yaml.events import AliasEvent\nprint(inspect.signature(Composer.compose_node))\nsrc=inspect.getsource(Composer.compose_node); print(src[:900])\nclass Rec(Composer):\n    def compose_node(self, parent, index):\n        if self.parser.check_event(AliasEvent):\n            ev=self.parser.peek_event()\n            self.loader.alias_log.append((type(parent).__name__, getattr(index,'value',index), ev.anchor, ev.start_mark.index, ev.end_mark.index))\n        return super().compose_node(parent, index)\ny=ruamel.yaml.YAML(); y.Composer=Rec; y.alias_log=[]\nt=\"d: &d {m: 1}\\nc:\\n  s: *d\\n  l: [*d, x]\\nmm:\\n  <<: *d\\n*d : 3\\n\"\ntry:\n    y.compose(t)\nexcept Exception as e: print(\"ERR\",type(e).__name__, e)\nprint(y.alias_log)\nEOF"}

tool: Write {"content": "# Design Document: config-aliases\n\n## Overview\nStep 6 makes theatres that share settings with YAML anchors usable, and safe to change (tracker issue #2). Anchors\n(`&name`), aliases (`*name`) and merge keys (`<<`) now load, and every command sees the **expanded\nconfiguration**. `troupe config set` changes a value defined at an anchor at the anchor, so the change appears\neverywhere the anchor is used. A `set` reached through an alias exits 3 and points to `--expand-alias`. With\nthat flag, the alias is replaced by a block-style copy marked `# expanded from *<name> by troupe`, and the value\nis set in the copy. Merge keys are never written.\n\nThe loader expands aliases and merge keys into the tree. Every **alias place** is its own node object marked\nwith an `AliasRef`, and its nodes keep the anchored node's origins. Every entry a mapping receives from a merge\nkey is marked `via_merge`. Readers need no change. The writer refuses or expands before planning, and writes at\nan anchor are replicated to every alias place by the written-location machinery of config-include-overrides\n(choice 18).\n\n### Goals\n- Load anchors, aliases and merge keys, with the exit-4 refusals (Req 1, 2). Every command reads the expanded\n  configuration (Req 3).\n- `set` at an anchor changes the anchor and keeps it (Req 4). `set` through an alias is refused (Req 5).\n- `--expand-alias` (Req 6) writes the copy exactly as specified (Req 7, 8). Merge keys are never written (Req 9).\n- Every write keeps the step 1 safety guarantees (Req 10).\n\n### Non-Goals\n- Writing merge keys, creating, renaming or removing anchors or aliases on request, and expanding every alias of\n  an anchor at once.\n- A VALUE with anchors or aliases (stays exit 2). Any change to rendering rules, events, lineage, reconcile or\n  `show` formats.\n\n## Boundary Commitments\n\n### This Spec Owns\n- Recording alias occurrences while composing, expanding aliases, merging merge keys, and the load refusals o...[truncated 29395 chars]

Accepted: configurations are small, local\n  and hand-edited, and the build is linear in the expanded size. No limit is added, because none is in the\n  contract. Revisit if theatres are ever loaded from untrusted sources.\n\n## References\"\"\",1)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nimport json\np='spec.json'; d=json.load(open(p))\nd['phase']='design-generated'; d['approvals']['design']={'generated':True,'approved':False}\nd['approvals']['requirements']['approved']=True; d['updated_at']='2026-10-06T05:00:00Z'\nopen(p,'w').write(json.dumps(d,indent=2)+\"\\n\")\nEOF\ncd /work/repo; wc -l spec/spec/config-aliases/design.md spec/spec/config-aliases/research.md; git add -A spec && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nspec(config-aliases): design an expanded tree with alias places, a write guard and in-memory expansion\n\nruamel composes an alias as the anchored node itself, so the alias's own\nposition is lost. A compose_node hook records each occurrence by parent and\nslot instead.\n\nAlias places are rebuilt per path with the anchor's origins and marked with\nAliasRef, so every reader sees the expanded value. A write at the anchor is\nreplicated to every alias place by the existing written-location machinery\n(step 5 choice 18).\n\nOne guard decides refuse, expand or proceed before the no-op check, which\nkeeps no-op sets through an ...[truncated 247 chars]
assistant: ## Status\nThe design is written to `spec/spec/config-aliases/design.md` (507 lines) and committed on `main` as `c9ec8ef`. `spec.json` is now at `design-generated`: design generated, not yet approved.\n\n## Discovery Type\nLight, because this extends existing code. A subagent analysed the loader, model, writeback and CLI, and I tested ruamel.yaml 0.19.1 directly.\n\n## Key Findings\n- **ruamel loses where an alias is written.** At an alias it hands back the anchored node itself, so the `*d` position is gone. Overriding `Composer.compose_node(parent, index)` records each occurrence exactly by parent and slot. It also catches aliases used as keys. Two more things the loader must now handle: recursive aliases compose without error, and a reused anchor name prints a warning to stderr.\n- **Writes at an anchor already have a mechanism.** The loader builds separate nodes for every place, and writes are keyed by where they are written in the file. Each alias place is therefore built again with the anchor's positions. Step 5's machinery for files included at several places then copies the anchor write to every alias place, and history gets an event for each path.\n- **The expansion runs in memory first.** `--expand-alias` expands one alias, reloads the configuration from memory, and repeats while aliases remain on PATH, outermost first. Then the normal `set` runs, with one atomic write and one read-back against the original files. The alias check runs before the "nothing to change" check, so a no-op `set` through an alias is still refused.\n\n## Review Gate\nPassed. All 51 requirement IDs are in the traceability table. Two local fixes were made: a missing allowed dependency, and the security note, which wasn't recorded in `research.md` yet.\n\n## Research Log\n`research.md` holds the investigation, 8 design decisions, records for all 17 approved choices, and the risks. One consequence comes from the design rather than a requirement: an anchored node that contains `<<` can't be copied without writing a merge key, so `--expand-alias` refuses it under 9.1.\n\n## Next Action\nReview the design, then:\n```\n/method-command config-aliases\n```\nUse `-y` to approve the design at the same time. `/method-command config-aliases` is an optional review before that.

tool: Write {"content": "# Implementation Plan\n\n**Conventions.** Every test written for this spec names the requirement IDs it proves in its docstring (for\nexample `\"\"\"Req 5.1: ...\"\"\"`), as in the earlier specs. Tests use real theatres on disk and the real CLI\n(`testing.md`). Preservation tests compare whole files byte for byte. Each task ends with\n`uv run pytest -n auto`, `uv run mypy --strict .`, `uv run ruff check .` and `uv run ruff format --check .`\npassing. Existing tests that pin behaviour a task changes are updated in that same task.\n\n**External prerequisites.** config-foundation, config-set-writeback, config-file-sync, config-get-show,\nconfig-lineage and config-include-overrides are complete. No new runtime dependency is added. ruamel.yaml stays\npinned at 0.19.1 (the composer hook depends on it).\n\n**Safety window.** From task 2.1 the loader accepts anchors, but `set` only learns to handle them in task 4.1.\nIn between, `set` refuses (exit 3, no file changed) any configuration that contains an anchor, an alias or a\nmerge key. Task 2.1 adds this temporary refusal, pinned by a test, and task 4.1 removes it together with its test.\nNo commit in between can write through an alias.\n\n- [ ] 1. Foundation: provenance model, merge-key merge and alias recording\n- [ ] 1.1 Record anchors, alias places and merge keys in the model, and provide the merge-key merge\n  - Every node can record the anchor written on it (name and the span of the `&name` property) and, at an alias place, the alias it stands for (name, the `*name` token's position, and the node as built at its anchor). A mapping can record the mapping as written behind its merge key. An entry can say that it came from a merge key. All new fields default to \"none\", so every existing construction and test is unchanged.\n  - The merge-key merge is pure. It builds a mapping's merged view from the mapping as written and its merge sources: own entries in written order, and at the place of the `<<` entry the...[truncated 17835 chars]

Today it drops them. An alias behind `$ref` overrides would then lose its marker, and a write could reach the anchor.\n  - **Refusal order:** target resolution refuses first, then the alias guard, then the planner.\n  - **Checking the expanded write:** the widened intended configuration comes from the expanded document, while verification runs against the original files.\n  - **Copy containing `<<`:** refused with the merge-key message plus the alias name. I also removed a wrong diagram edge.\n\n**What the second review found, now fixed in the draft:**\n1. After 2.1 accepts anchors, an alias would load silently as a plain copy before 2.2 builds alias places. 2.1 now refuses aliases on load (exit 4) until 2.2 removes that refusal.\n2. The temporary `set` refusal had no test for a file that uses only an inline `<<`. 2.3 now pins one.\n3. A grep over all of `tests/` can't check traceability, because earlier specs use the same `Req N.M` numbers. The check is now limited to this spec's own test files.\n4. The design and task 3.6 gave different messages for the refusal to copy a node that holds `<<`. Both now use the merge-key message.\n5. 3.6 also needs `AliasSite` from 3.3, so it now depends on 3.3 as well.\n6. 3.4 lost its `(P)`, since it depends on 3.1.\n\n**Plan shape:** 5 groups, 16 sub-tasks, and all 51 requirement IDs covered. Three tasks are marked `(P)`: 1.2, 3.2 and 3.3. A temporary refusal stops `set` writing through an alias or merge key between 2.1 and 4.1.\n\n**Next:** to approve the design clarifications, re-run `/method-command config-aliases`. assistant: ## Status\nThe task plan is written to `spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md` and committed on `main` as `8514a80`, with `spec.json` at `tasks-generated`. That commit also includes the design clarifications from the last run. They are recorded in `research.md` as found during planning, with no behaviour change. The main one: `deep_merge` must keep alias, anchor and merge-key information, otherwise aliases behind `$ref` overrides would escape the alias check.\n\n## Task Summary\n5 major tasks and 18 sub-tasks, each sized at 1–3 hours, covering all 51 requirements:\n1. **Foundation (1.1–1.2):** the model fields, the merge-key merge, and the composer that records where each alias is written.\n2. **Loading (2.1–2.4):** anchors, then aliases, then merge keys, then a check that every command reads the expanded configuration.\n3. **File layer (3.1–3.7):** layout, copying changes to every alias place, the alias check, keys next to `<<`, the two planner refusals, and the copy written by `--expand-alias`.\n4. **`set` (4.1–4.4):** the refusals, writes at anchors, the expansion step, and the CLI flag.\n5. **Validation (5.1–5.2):** the issue #2 scenario byte for byte, then the notes in the earlier specs and the traceability check.\n\nTasks 1.2, 3.2 and 3.3 can run in parallel.\n\nAnchors start loading in 2.1, but the alias check only arrives in 4.1. Until then a temporary refusal, pinned by tests, stops `set` from writing through an alias or merge key.\n\n## Quality Validation\n- Every requirement is mapped to a task, and design coverage and dependencies were checked.\n- The task-plan review gate passed.\n- The task-graph sanity review, run by an independent subagent, returned `PASS` after one repair round. Its four minor suggestions are folded in.\n- Testing is part of every task, and each task names its test file.\n\n## Next Action\nTasks generated. Approve and proceed to implementation?

An anchored node's ``origin.span`` excludes its property. Keys never carry\nanchors.\n\nThe loader expands every alias ``*name`` into the anchored node built again for its path: an **alias\nplace**. Its root records ``alias: AliasRef`` (the name, the ``*name`` token as written and the node\nas built at its anchor). An alias place and its descendants carry the anchored node's origins; the\nalias place's own written text is ``alias.token``.\n\nA mapping with a merge key (a plain ``<<`` key) is the **merged view** of the mapping as written\n(``troupe.config.merge_keys.merge_mapping``): its own entries in written order, and at the place of\nthe ``<<`` entry the keys of the merge sources that it does not write itself, earlier sources first.\nIts ``merge: MergeKey`` keeps the mapping as written (with its ``<<`` entry) and the ``<<`` key's\norigin. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of the recording composer (task 1.2): ``troupe.config.compose.compose_file``.\n\nEach case writes a real file, reads it back as a ``SourceFile`` and composes it. The loader still refuses anchors at this point (task 2.1), so the\nend-to-end cases only check that composing reports and stays silent as before.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport warnings\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\nfrom ruamel.yaml.nodes import MappingNode, Node, ScalarNode, SequenceNode\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre, RunCli\nfrom troupe.config.compose import AliasOccurrence, ComposedFile, compose_file\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError\nfrom troupe.config.model import LineEnding, SourceFile, Span\n\n\ndef _source(tmp_path: Path, text: str, line_ending: LineEnding = LineEnding.LF) -> SourceFile:\n    path = tmp_path / \"troupe.yml\"\n    path.write_bytes(text.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n    decoded = path.read_bytes().decode(\"utf-8\")\n    return SourceFile(\n        path=path, real_path=path.resolve(), text=decoded, line_ending=line_ending\n    )\n\n\ndef _compose(tmp_path: Path, text: str) -> tuple[SourceFile, ComposedFile]:\n    source = _source(tmp_path, text)\n    return source, compose_file(source)\n\n\ndef _mapping(node: Node | None) -> MappingNode:\n    assert isinstance(node, MappingNode)\n    return node\n\n\ndef _sequence(node: Node) -> SequenceNode:\n    assert isinstance(node, SequenceNode)\n    return node\n\n\ndef _entry(mapping: MappingNode, key: str) -> tuple[ScalarNode, Node]:\n    for key_node, value_node in mapping.value:\n        if isinstance(key_node, ScalarNode) and key_node.value == key:\n            retu...[truncated 9865 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Compose one YAML file and record every alias occurrence (config-aliases Requirement 1).\n\nruamel.yaml's round-trip composer returns the anchored node itself at each alias, so the composed\ntree cannot tell an alias place from its anchor and keeps no position of the ``*name`` token. The\nrecording composer hooks ``Composer.compose_node(parent, index)`` and records each alias before\nruamel resolves it, keyed by the parent node and the slot the alias fills (research.md, Decision 1):\n\n- the key node's ``id`` for a mapping value,\n- the item index for a list item,\n- ``None`` for an alias used as a mapping key.\n\nKeys use ``id()`` of composed nodes, which live as long as :attr:`ComposedFile.root`. An anchored\nnode is composed once, so the aliases inside it are recorded once, under that node, and are found\nagain wherever the node is built through an alias. Each record is checked against the source text,\nwhich must read ``*<name>`` at the recorded span.\n\nA reused anchor name is valid YAML (each alias refers to the most recent definition, 1.2), so\nruamel's ``ReusedAnchorWarning`` is silenced, around the compose call only. Syntax errors, several\ndocuments and undefined aliases (1.3) are :class:`ConfigLoadError` naming the file.\n\nNothing is ever created, modified, renamed or deleted.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport warnings\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom typing import TypeAlias\n\nfrom ruamel.yaml import YAML\nfrom ruamel.yaml.composer import Composer\nfrom ruamel.yaml.error import MarkedYAMLError, ReusedAnchorWarning, YAMLError\nfrom ruamel.yaml.events import AliasEvent\nfrom ruamel.yaml.nodes import Node\n\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ConfigLoadError\nfrom troupe.config.model import SourceFile, Span\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass AliasOccurrence:\n    \"\"\"One alias as written: its name and the span of its ``*name`` token.\"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    span: Span\n\n\nAliasSlot: TypeAlias = tuple[int...[truncated 4014 chars]

Task 2.1: an anchor ``&name``\non any value node is accepted and recorded as ``Anchor(name, span of the property)``, and the\nnode's own span is its value without the property (research.md, Decision 3): the first key for a\nblock mapping, the first ``-`` for a block list, the next token for scalars and flow collections,\nand empty at the property's end for an empty null. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Anchors, aliases and merge keys end to end through the real ``troupe`` console script\n(config-aliases).\n\nTask 2.1: a file with an anchor is read like any other file (the anchor-only row that\nconfig-foundation refused with exit 4), and an anchor on a mapping key is still refused, with no\nhistory state created.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import CliResult, MakeTheatre, RunCli\n\nCONFIG_NAMES = [\"troupe.yml\", \"troupe.yaml\"]\n\n\ndef _get(run_cli: RunCli, theatre: Path, path: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"get\", path, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _no_history_state(theatre: Path) -> bool:\n    return not list((theatre / \".troupe\").glob(\"history.sqlite*\"))\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"config_name\", CONFIG_NAMES)\ndef test_get_reads_an_anchored_scalar(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, run_cli: RunCli, config_name: str\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 1.1, 1.6: ``a: &x 1`` loads; ``get a`` prints the anchored value, without the anchor,\n    and the file is unchanged.\"\"\"\n    content = \"a: &x 1\\n\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(content, config_name=config_name)\n\n    result = _get(run_cli, theatre, \"a\")\n\n    assert (result.exit_code, result.stdout, result.stderr) == (0, b\"1\\n\", \"\")\n    assert (theatre / \".troupe\" / config_name).read_bytes() == content.encode()\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"config_name\", CONFIG_NAMES)\ndef test_get_reads_values_inside_anchored_collections(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre, run_cli: RunCli, config_name: str\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 1.1, 1.5: values inside an anchored block mapping, an anchored list and an anchored\n    flow mapping are read at their paths.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(\n        \"defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\nlist: &l\\n  - one\\nflow: &f {k: 'v'}\\n\",\n        config_name=config_name,\n    )\n\n    results ...[truncated 1163 chars]

&x 5\\n\",\n \"tag-after\": \"a: &x !!str 5\\n\",\n \"verbatim\": \"a: !<tag:yaml.org,2002:str> &x 5\\n\",\n \"local-tag-map\": \"a: !foo &x\\n  k: 1\\n\",\n \"tag-other-node\": \"a: !!str 5\\nb: &x 6\\n\",\n \"tag-flow-neighbour\": \"a: [!!str 5, &x 6]\\n\",\n \"empty-tag-then-anchor-item\": \"a:\\n- !!null\\n- &x 6\\n\",\n \"block-map\": \"a: &x  # c\\n  # d\\n  k: 1\\n\",\n \"block-list\": \"a: &x\\n  - 1\\n\",\n \"block-list-compact\": \"a: &x\\n- 1\\n\",\n \"scalar\": \"a: &x   'q'\\n\",\n \"flow\": \"a: &x {k: 1}\\n\",\n \"null\": \"a: &x\\nb: 1\\n\",\n \"null-comment\": \"a: &x  # c\\nb: 1\\n\",\n \"null-flow\": \"a: {k: &x , j: 1}\\n\",\n \"null-flow2\": \"a: [&x , 1]\\n\",\n \"root\": \"&r\\nk: 1\\n\",\n \"literal\": \"a: &x |\\n  t\\n\",\n \"hash-name\": \"a: &x#c 5\\n\",\n \"key-anchor-flow\": \"a: {&k b: 1}\\n\",\n \"alias-key\": \"a: &x k\\nb:\\n  *x : 1\\n\",\n}\nfor name, text in cases.items():\n    p = Path(f\"/tmp/r21/{name}.yaml\"); p.write_text(text)\n    try:\n        d = load_config(p)\n        def walk(n, path=\"a\"):\n            from troupe.config.model import ConfigMapping, ConfigSequence\n            out=[]\n            if n.anchor: out.append((path, n.anchor.name, text[n.anchor.span.start:n.anchor.span.end], repr(text[n.origin.span.start:n.origin.span.end])))\n            if isinstance(n, ConfigMapping):\n                for e in n.entries: out+=walk(e.value, path+\".\"+e.key)\n            if isinstance(n, ConfigSequence):\n                for i,it in enumerate(n.items): out+=walk(it, f\"{path}[{i}]\")\n            return out\n        print(name, \"OK\", walk(d.root, \"<root>\"))\n    except ConfigLoadError as e:\n        print(name, \"ERR\", e)\nEOF\ncd /work/repo && uv run python /tmp/r21/t.py"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"- [ ] 2.1 Accept anchors on values\",\"- [x] 2.1 Accept anchors on values\",1)\ns+=\"- 2.1: an anchor written on an include string or a `$ref` mapping is recorded on the included node, but its `Anchor.span` lies in the **including** file (the file of `include.directive`), not in the node's `origin` file. Layout (3.1) and planner (3.5) must use the directive's file for such anchors. Tags next to an anchor are detected by a token scan (`_tags_next_to_anchors`), because ruamel starts an anchored node's marks at the anchor. The interim alias-as-key refusal names the mapping's path; 2.2 owns the final message. The temporary `set` refusal sits at the top of `set_value` and walks `layers`, `include.form` and `merge.written`.\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/loader.py src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py tests/test_compose.py tests/test_e2e_includes.py tests/test_e2e_locate_load.py tests/test_loader.py tests/test_loader_includes.py tests/test_e2e_aliases.py tests/test_loader_aliases.py tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(config-aliases): accept anchors on values, with value spans that exclude the property\n\nTheatres that share settings with anchors could not be read at all (decision\nH). Anchors on values now load. The node's span leaves out the `&name`\nproperty, so later write rules that start at the value keep the anchor.\nAnchors on keys and tags written next to an anchor stay refused.\n\nTwo guards keep this step safe on its own:\n- Every alias is a load error until alias places exist (2.2). Otherwise it\n  would silently load as a plain copy.\n- `set` refuses any configuration with an anchor, alias or merge key until\n  the write guard exists (4.1), so no commit in between can reproduce\n  issue #2.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1"}

Every consumer (layout 3.1, guard 3.3, `anchor_uses` and the 4.6 planner check, expander) must check `alias` before `anchor`, or it will take an alias place for a second anchored node. `$ref: *i` is accepted. The alias is recorded only on the `$ref` scalar inside `include.form` (not on the included node), so the guard must walk `include.form` to see it.\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/loader.py src/troupe/config/overrides.py tests/test_loader_aliases.py tests/test_e2e_aliases.py tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py tests/test_loader.py tests/test_e2e_locate_load.py tests/test_compose.py spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(config-aliases): build alias places in the expanded configuration\n\nEvery reader should see what YAML means, so each alias is now built as its\nown place holding the anchored node's content. Its origins point at the\nanchor's written spans, which later lets a write at the anchor be copied to\nevery alias place by written location.\n\nEach place records its alias, so the writer can refuse or expand writes\nthrough it. deep_merge keeps that provenance behind $ref overrides, so an\nalias stays visible even when an override is merged over it.\n\nAn alias used as a key or inside its own anchored node is refused, as is an\nundefined alias. The temporary set refusal still blocks every configuration\nwith aliases until the write guard exists (4.1).\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1; git status --short"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport pathlib\np=pathlib.Path(\"src/troupe/config/loader.py\"); t=p.read_text()\ns=t.index(\"    def _merge_sources(\")\ne=t.index(\"    def _ref_value(\")\nt=t[:s]+'''    def _merge_sources(self, value: ConfigNode, key_path: ConfigPath) -> tuple[ConfigMapping, ...]:\n        \"\"\"Return the mappings a merge key's ``value`` merges: the mapping (or alias place of a\n        mapping) itself, or each item of a list of those; refuse any other value naming the merge\n        key's path (config-aliases 2.3).\"\"\"\n        if isinstance(value, ConfigMapping):\n            return (value,)\n        if isinstance(value, ConfigScalar):\n            raise self._bad_merge_value(\"found a scalar\", key_path)\n        if value.alias is not None:\n            raise self._bad_merge_value(\"found an alias of a list\", key_path)\n        sources: list[ConfigMapping] = []\n        for index, item in enumerate(value.items):\n            if not isinstance(item, ConfigMapping):\n                kind = \"a scalar\" if isinstance(item, ConfigScalar) else \"a list\"\n                raise self._bad_merge_value(f\"item {index} is {kind}\", key_path)\n            sources.append(item)\n        return tuple(sources)\n\n    def _bad_merge_value(self, found: str, key_path: ConfigPath) -> ConfigLoadError:\n        return ConfigLoadError(\n            f\"{_MERGE_VALUE_NEEDED}; {found}\", file=self._source.path, path=_text(key_path)\n        )\n\n'''+t[e:]\np.write_text(t)\nEOF\nuv run ruff format . >/dev/null; uv run ruff check . && uv run mypy --strict . 2>&1 | tail -2; uv run pytest -q tests/test_loader_merge_keys.py tests/test_e2e_aliases.py 2>&1 | tail -2"}

tool: SendMessage {"content": "Task 2.3 review round 1: REJECTED, with one Impor…", "message": "Task 2.3 review round 1: REJECTED, with one Important finding. Fix it and report again with the full `## Status Report` block, including RED_PHASE_OUTPUT for the new tests.\n\nFinding (your concern 2): includes are silently accepted as `<<` merge sources. The reviewer confirmed these all load and give `via_merge` entries:\n- `<<: \"file:base.yml\"`\n- `<<: [\"file:base.yml\"]`\n- `<<: {$ref: \"file:base.yml\", a: 9}`\n- an alias of an anchored include string (`w: {<<: &q \"file:base.yml\"}` / `v: {<<: *q}`)\n\nThis violates Requirement 2.3 and Boundary choice 15, because an include string is written as a scalar. It also breaks research Decision 4, which says not to combine two merge mechanisms, and the conservative-default rule.\n\nRemediation:\n- In `_merge_sources` (or before the `<<` value is built), refuse with ConfigLoadError (exit 4) any merge source that is an include, whether it is the `<<` value or a list item. That covers:\n  - a scalar include string;\n  - a `$ref` mapping, with or without overrides;\n  - an alias place of an anchored include string or `$ref` mapping.\n  The check is: the built node has `include` set.\n- The message names the file and the `<<` key path. Reuse the `_MERGE_VALUE_NEEDED` prefix with a \"found an include\" detail.\n- Keep accepted: a plain mapping, or an alias of one, whose nested values contain includes.\n- Add loader tests in tests/test_loader_merge_keys.py for each refused form (message, file, key path), and add one e2e row in tests/test_e2e_aliases.py (exit 4, no history state).\n- Update the loader module docstring's merge-key paragraph to say includes are refused as merge sources.\n\nThe other concerns (1, 3, 4, 5, 6) are approved as they are. Re-run the targeted files and mypy/ruff. A full-suite run is optional; I will run it myself. assistant: I fixed the review finding: an include used as a `<<` merge source is now refused with exit 4, naming the file and the `<<` key path. This covers an include used directly as the `<<` value or as a list item. A plain mapping, or an alias of one, whose nested values are includes still merges. The targeted test files, mypy strict and ruff all pass; I did not re-run the full suite, as you said you would.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 2.3\n- TASK_BRIEF: A plain `<<` whose value is a mapping, an alias of a mapping or a list of those is merged into its mapping. Own keys stay in written order; merged keys take the place of `<<`, earlier sources first, marked `via_merge`. No `<<` key is loaded, and the mapping as written is kept in `merge`. Exit 4, naming the file and the `<<` key path, for: any other value; any source that is an include (include string, `$ref` mapping with or without overrides, or an alias of either); and a `<<` among `$ref` overrides. A quoted `<<` stays an ordinary key. `deep_merge` keeps `merge` and the merged entries' marks. The `<<` text has one named home. tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; git status --short; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"- [ ] 2.3 Load merge keys\",\"- [x] 2.3 Load merge keys\",1)\ns+=\"- 2.3: `MERGE_KEY` lives in `troupe/config/constants.py`, its single home. Includes (include strings, `$ref` mappings, aliases of either) are refused as merge sources (exit 4); a source's *nested* values may be includes. `<<: *list` is refused and `<<: []` merges nothing. A merged entry reused by `deep_merge` keeps `via_merge=True` even when an override deep-merges into it, so the guard (3.3) treats it as reached through a merge key, which is conservative.\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/constants.py src/troupe/config/loader.py src/troupe/config/overrides.py src/troupe/config/yaml_style.py tests/test_loader_merge_keys.py tests/test_e2e_aliases.py tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py tests/test_loader.py tests/test_loader_includes.py tests/test_e2e_locate_load.py tests/test_merge_keys.py spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(config-aliases): load merge keys into the merged view\n\nThe step 6 contract names merge keys, so files that use them must load\n(requirements choice 1). A plain `<<` merges its mapping sources the way YAML\ndefines it. The mapping as written is kept behind the merged view, so the\nwriter can reach the physical entries and never write `<<`.\n\nIncludes are refused as merge sources (exit 4). Combining the `$ref` merge with\nthe merge-key merge has no defined meaning, so refusing is the conservative\nchoice. The `<<` text now has one home in constants.py.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1; git status --short"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"- [ ] 3.1 Teach the file layout\",\"- [x] 3.1 Teach the file layout\",1)\ns+=\"- 3.1: `FileLayout.entry_slot` raises `ValueError` for an entry with `via_merge`. The guard (3.3) and target/planner (3.4) must refuse or resolve to own entries before asking the layout for a slot. An anchor on a scalar include is found as the `&name` text right before the directive in the including file. `_block_scalar_key_columns` still loops over an alias place's own entries, which is harmless because they keep the anchored node's columns.\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/overrides.py src/troupe/config/writeback/layout.py tests/writeback/test_layout_aliases.py spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(config-aliases): teach the file layout about anchor properties, alias places and merge-key mappings\n\nWrite rules find extents, entry ends, insertion points, comments and the\nindentation step by walking the nodes written in a file. Three things now\nfit that walk:\n- An anchored value starts after its `&name` property, so every existing\n  replacement keeps the anchor.\n- An alias place is a leaf at its `*name` token, not a second copy of the\n  anchor's text.\n- A mapping with `<<` is walked as written.\nFiles without these constructs lay out exactly as before.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1"}

Fix it and report again with the full `## Status Report` block, including RED_PHASE_OUTPUT for any new test.\n\nFinding (your concern 1): `_merge_key` in shared.py copies `merge_mapping`'s merge-key precedence and order rule. The design (Components and Interfaces → Shared) says `_Rebuild` re-merges \"with `merge_mapping`\" and declares the Shared → MergeKeys dependency, so the rule's owner is merge_keys.py. With two homes, read-back verification (10.2) could silently disagree with the loader. The `deep_merge` precedent does not apply, because config-include-overrides explicitly allowed that copy and this spec does not.\n\nRemediation (approved by the controller; touching src/troupe/config/merge_keys.py is within the declared Shared → MergeKeys dependency):\n- Give the rule one home in `troupe/config/merge_keys.py`: a small, pure, generic helper (typed with a TypeVar for the value) that takes the mapping's own keys and the ordered sources' (key, value) pairs, and returns the pairs to merge in, earlier sources first, skipping own keys and keys already taken.\n- Make both `merge_mapping` (on MappingEntry) and `shared._Rebuild` (on plain values) use it, and delete the copied logic in `_merge_key`. A thin adapter in shared.py that only calls the helper is fine.\n- Keep tests/test_merge_keys.py and tests/writeback/test_shared_aliases.py passing, and add a unit test for the helper in tests/test_merge_keys.py.\n\nThe other concerns (2, 3) are acceptable as they are. Re-run the targeted files and mypy/ruff. A full-suite run is optional; I will run it myself. Do NOT edit tasks.md and do NOT commit.", "recipient": "a3e0b790748138c96", "summary": "Task 3.2 remediation round 1", "to": "a3e0b790748138c96", "type": "message"}

This is the single home of the merge-key\n    precedence and order rule; the loader's merged view and the writer's rebuilt plain view both\n    use it. assistant: I fixed the review finding: the merge-key rule now lives only in `src/troupe/config/merge_keys.py`, and the loader and the writer both call it. The targeted test files, mypy and ruff pass; I did not re-run the full suite, as you said you will. Nothing is committed and `tasks.md` is unchanged.\n\n- **The single rule:** a new pure function `merged_pairs[V](own, sources)` in merge_keys.py returns the pairs to merge in. Earlier sources come first, each in its order, and own keys and keys already taken are skipped.\n- **Loader side:** `merge_mapping` now calls `merged_pairs` on entries and then marks them `via_merge=True`. Its behaviour is unchanged, and the existing tests in `tests/test_merge_keys.py` still pass.\n- **Writer side:** the copied logic in `_merge_key` in shared.py is gone. It now only keeps own entries in order and, at the `<<` entry, adds whatever `merged_pairs` returns for the plain sources. A small `_source` helper unwraps each source to a mapping.\n- **New test:** `test_merged_pairs_skip_own_keys_and_keys_of_earlier_sources` in `tests/test_merge_keys.py` covers own-key and earlier-source precedence, order, no sources, empty sources, and that the inputs are not changed.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 3.2\n- TASK_BRIEF: Rebuilding the intended configuration from written locations re-applies merge keys, so a changed value, an added key and a removed key at an anchor appear at the anchor, at every plain alias and in every `<<: *d` mapping, except where that mapping writes the key itself. The merge-key precedence and order rule has one home in merge_keys.py, used by both `merge_mapping` and `_Rebuild`.\n- FILES_CHANGED: src/troupe/config/writeback/shared.py, src/troupe/config/merge_keys.py, tests/writeback/test_shared_aliases.py, tests/test_merge_keys.py\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 10.2 (the helper test also names 2.1, 2.2)\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: Components and Interfaces -> File layer -> Shared (`shared.py`, re-merge through MergeKeys); MergeKeys (`merge_mapping`); research.md Decision 2\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: Round 1: `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_shared_aliases.py` gave 7 failed, 8 passed. Example: "('reviewer', [('model','opus'),('t',2)]) != ('reviewer', [('model','sonnet'),('t',2)])". The 8 that already passed are kept as pins. Round 2 (new helper test): `uv run pytest -q tests/test_merge_keys.py` gave "ImportError: cannot import name 'merged_pairs' from 'troupe.config.merge_keys'" (1 error during collection).\n- TESTS_RUN: `uv run pytest -n auto -q tests/test_merge_keys.py tests/writeback/test_shared_aliases.py tests/writeback/test_shared_placement.py tests/test_loader_merge_keys.py tests/test_loader_aliases.py tests/test_e2e_aliases.py`: 239 passed. `uv run mypy --strict .`: no issues in 148 source files. `uv run ruff check .`: all checks passed. `uv run ruff format --check .`: 149 files already formatted. The full suite was run in round 1 only (3516 passed) and not again after this refactor.\n- CONCERNS: The tests record `WrittenChanges` by hand on loaded documents, because the planner learns anchors in tasks 3.4 and 3.5. The reach check stays as it was: a change recorded at the anchor is still refused when PATH runs through a merged entry, which is conservative, and the guard (task 3.3) refuses such a PATH first anyway.\n- EVIDENCE: `merge_keys.merged_pairs` (generic over the value type, pure) is the only implementation of the rule. `merge_mapping` calls it on `(entry.key, entry)` pairs and marks the results `via_merge=True`. `shared._Rebuild._content` rebuilds a mapping with `merge` through `_merge_key(self._entries(node.merge.written))`, which calls `merged_pairs` at the `<<` entry. Tests: test_merged_pairs_skip_own_keys_and_keys_of_earlier_sources, the existing merge_mapping tests, and the 15 tests in test_shared_aliases.py (changed value, shadowed value, added key in merged order, removed key with and without a shadowing own key, list append, several and nested sources, an included file, an own-key change, and 6 no-change pins).

The copied logic is gone, which matches design.md › Shared's requirement to re-merge using the MergeKeys rule.\n  2. FYI. tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"- [ ] 3.2 (P) Replicate changes\",\"- [x] 3.2 (P) Replicate changes\",1)\ns+=\"- 3.2: the merge-key precedence rule lives only in `merge_keys.merged_pairs`, which both `merge_mapping` (on entries) and `shared._Rebuild` (on plain values) use. Alias places needed no rebuild change, because they share the anchored node's written locations. The reach check still refuses a change at an anchor when PATH runs through a merged entry, which is conservative; the guard (3.3) refuses that PATH first.\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/merge_keys.py src/troupe/config/writeback/shared.py tests/test_merge_keys.py tests/writeback/test_shared_aliases.py spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(config-aliases): replicate changes through merge keys when building the intended configuration\n\nRead-back verification compares the files with an intended configuration\nrebuilt from written locations. Alias places already share the anchor's\nlocations, but mappings with `<<` were rebuilt from their merged view, so a\nchange at an anchor never reached them. Verification would then wrongly\nrefuse a correct anchor write.\n\nSuch mappings are now rebuilt from the mapping as written and merged again.\nThe merge-key precedence rule moves into merge_keys.merged_pairs, so the\nloader and verification cannot disagree about what a merge key means.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1"}

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of the alias guard: refuse, expand or proceed for a set (config-aliases task 3.3).\n\nThe guard (``troupe.config.writeback.aliases``) walks PATH from the root to its node (or to the\ndeepest existing ancestor of a PATH that does not exist yet) and, for a collection replacement, the\nchanged parts of the subtree at PATH. The first alias place met gives an expansion site with\n``--expand-alias`` and the alias refusal without it; the first merged entry met gives the\nmerge-key refusal either way; nothing met means proceed. ``anchor_uses`` indexes every anchored\nnode's written location with the aliases that use it.\n\nEach case loads a real theatre configuration from disk and resolves PATH with the real\nTargetResolver; every refusal's message is asserted exactly.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ExitCode, WriteRefusedError\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigDocument, ConfigMapping, ConfigNode, ConfigSequence\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.aliases import AliasSite, alias_sites, anchor_uses\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.shared import written_location\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\n\nSHARED = (\n    \"defaults: &d\\n\"\n    \"  model: opus\\n\"\n    \"  t: 1\\n\"\n    \"coder:\\n\"\n    \"  settings: *d\\n\"\n    \"  name: c\\n\"\n    \"reviewer:\\n\"\n    \"  <<: *d\\n\"\n    \"  t: 2\\n\"\n)\n\"\"\"`defaults` is anchored as `&d`, used by a plain alias (`coder.settings`) and by a merge key with\na shadowing own key `t` (`reviewer`).\"\"\"\n\nNESTED = (\n    \"base: &e\\n\"\n    \"  x: 1\\n\"\n    \"defaults: &d\\n\"\n    \"  inner: *e\\n\"\n    \"  y: 2\\n\"\n    \"coder:\\n\"\n    \"  ...[truncated 17116 chars]

For a replacement it then\nsearches the subtree at PATH, but only the alias places and merged entries whose plain value the\nintended configuration changes or removes, in document order and never below an alias place (outer\naliases come first, 8.1, 8.2). The first one met decides:\n\n* a merged entry (a value a mapping receives from a merge key) is refused with\n  :data:`MERGE_REFUSAL` whether or not ``--expand-alias`` is given (9.2);\n* an alias place is the :class:`AliasSite` to expand with ``--expand-alias``, and otherwise is\n  refused with :data:`ALIAS_REFUSAL` (5.1, 5.2);\n* nothing met means proceed: ``()`` (6.3).\n\nWhere an alias is seen. An alias place records ``alias`` on its root (which also carries the\nanchored node's ``anchor``, so ``alias`` is checked first). A merged mapping (an include with\noverrides) keeps its included side's provenance, so its override side is looked at as well. An alias\nused as a ``$ref`` value is recorded only on the ``$ref`` scalar inside ``include.form``; that scalar\nis the alias place of the include root holding the form.\n\nThe guard runs after target resolution's own refusals and before any planner refusal, and before\nthe no-op check, so a no-op through an alias is refused too (5.3).\n\n:func:`anchor_uses` indexes the aliases of the configuration by the written location of the\nanchored node they stand for (used by the planner's check for removing a used anchor, 4.6).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom collections.abc import Iterator, Mapping\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom typin...[truncated 13930 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/probe_test.py <<'EOF'\nfrom support import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.aliases import alias_sites\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.errors import WriteRefusedError\n\ndef run(doc, p, v, expand):\n    cp = ConfigPath.parse(p)\n    req = resolve_target(doc, cp, parse_value(v, cp))\n    try:\n        s = alias_sites(doc, req, expand=expand)\n        return [str(x.path) for x in s]\n    except WriteRefusedError as e:\n        return \"REFUSED \" + e.problem[:50]\n\ndef test_probe(make_theatre: MakeTheatre):\n    d = load_config(make_theatre(\"i: &i file:inc.yml\\nr:\\n  $ref: *i\\n  j: 2\\n\", files={\".troupe/inc.yml\": \"k: v\\n\"}) / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n    print(\"r.j\", run(d, \"r.j\", \"3\", False), run(d, \"r.j\", \"3\", True))\n    d2 = load_config(make_theatre(\"d: &d\\n  q: 2\\nr:\\n  $ref: file:inc.yml\\n  x: *d\\n\", files={\".troupe/inc.yml\": \"x:\\n  p: 1\\n\"}) / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n    print(\"r.x.p\", run(d2, \"r.x.p\", \"3\", False), run(d2, \"r.x.p\", \"3\", True))\n    # merge via override side: included has m with <<\n    d3 = load_config(make_theatre(\"d: &d\\n  a: 1\\nr:\\n  $ref: file:inc.yml\\n  m:\\n    b: 2\\n\", files={\".troupe/inc.yml\": \"d: &d\\n  a: 1\\nm:\\n  <<: *d\\n  c: 3\\n\"}) / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n    print(\"r.m.a\", run(d3, \"r.m.a\", \"5\", False), \"r.m.b\", run(d3, \"r.m.b\", \"5\", False), \"r.m.c\", run(d3, \"r.m.c\", \"5\", False))\n    # list alias item\n    d4 = load_config(make_theatre(\"d: &d\\n  a: 1\\nl:\\n  - *d\\n  - z\\n\") / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\")\n    print(\"l.0.a\", run(d4, \"l.0.a\", \"5\", False), \"l.1\", run(d4, \"l.1\", \"y\", False), \"l replace\", run(d4, \"l\", \"[{a: 1}, w]\", False), run(d4, \"l\", \"[{a: 2}, w]\", True))...[truncated 503 chars]
tool: SendMessage {"content": "Task 3.3 review round 1: REJECTED, with one Impor…", "message": "Task 3.3 review round 1: REJECTED, with one Important finding. Fix it and report again with the full `## Status Report` block, including RED_PHASE_OUTPUT for the new tests.\n\nFinding 1 (Important, your concern 1): `_site` and `_form_site` treat a whole include root, and both sides of a merged mapping, as alias places for every PATH through them. The reviewer probed this on loaded documents:\n- `i: &i file:inc.yml` with `r: {$ref: *i, j: 2}`: `r.j` is refused, and with the flag gives site `r.$ref`. But `j` is written in r's own override, not reached through `*i`.\n- `r: {$ref: file:inc.yml, x: *d}`, with the included file holding `x: {p: 1}`: `r.x.p` is refused naming `*d`, and with the flag gives site `r.x`. But `p` comes from inc.yml.\n\nThis contradicts the requirements term \"Reached through an alias\" (\"Values written in the mapping itself are not\"). With the flag it also breaks 6.3, because it expands an alias that is not on PATH. The narrowing is not recorded either (collaboration.md: do not narrow scope quietly).\n\nRemediation:\n- Make the walk side-aware.\n  - At an include root whose `$ref` value is an alias, count that alias only when PATH ends at the include root, or when the next segment's value comes from the included side. A key written only in the overrides is not reached through it. A key that both sides write as mappings is followed on both sides.\n  - At a merged mapping (`layers`), check `_site` only on the side or sides that hold the next PATH segment's value. Check both sides only when PATH ends at that node.\n  - Apply the same rule in `_Below.search`: count an aliased `$ref`, or an alias on a merged side, only when a value that changes is on that side.\n- Add tests in tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py:\n  - `r.j` (an override key behind `$ref: *i`) proceeds, with and without the flag;\n  - `r.x.p` (the included side of a merged mapping whose override is `*d`) p...[truncated 986 chars]

The location of an anchored include is where its anchor is written: the\n    include string, or the `$ref` mapping.\"\"\"\n    document = _document(\n        make_theatre,\n        \"e: &e 1\\\\n\"\n        \"d: &d\\\\n\"\n        \"  inner: *e\\\\n\"\n        \"a: *d\\\\n\"\n        \"m:\\\\n\"\n        \"  <<: *d\\\\n\"\n        \"l:\\\\n\"\n        \"  - *d\\\\n\"\n        \"unused: &u 3\\\\n\"\n        \"i: &i file:inc.yml\\\\n\"\n        \"x: *i\\\\n\"\n        \"r:\\\\n\"\n        \"  $ref: *i\\\\n\"\n        \"  j: 2\\\\n\"\n        \"f: &a {$ref: file:inc.yml, j: 3}\\\\n\"\n        \"g: *a\\\\n\",\n        files={\".troupe/inc.yml\": \"k: v\\\\n\"},\n    )\n    uses = anchor_uses(document)\n    text = document.root_file.text\n\n    def written(location: tuple[Path, int, int]) -> str:\n        assert location[0] == document.root_file.real_path\n        return text[location[1] : location[2]]\n\n    def tokens(location: tuple[Path, int, int]) -> list[tuple[str, int]]:\n        return [(text[ref.token.span.start : ref.token.span.end], ref.token.span.start)\n                for ref in uses[location]]\n\n    e, d...[truncated 4299 chars]

A merged mapping (an\n    include with overrides) is split into its sides, and only the side or sides that hold the\n    next key are followed (a key both sides write as mappings is followed on both, as\n    ``deep_merge`` merges it; a key the override writes otherwise is the override's). An alias place records ``alias`` on its root (which also carries the\nanchored node's ``anchor``, so ``alias`` is checked first). The walk follows the nodes as written\n(see :class:`_Guard`): a merged mapping (an include with overrides) only repeats its included side's\nprovenance, so it is split into its sides, and only the sides that hold the next key of PATH are\nfollowed; where PATH ends, every side counts. An alias used as a ``$ref`` value is recorded only on\nthe ``$ref`` scalar inside ``include.form``; it counts for the include's included side only, so a\nkey written only in the include's own overrides is not reached through it (requirements, \"reached\nthrough an alias\": values written in the mapping itself are not).\n\n'''\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\np='spec/spec/config-aliases/research.md'\ns=open(p).read()\nold='''- **Rationale**: one place for \"reached through an alias\". It runs before the no-op check, so 5.3 and 8.6 hold.\n'''\nnew=old+'''- **Implementation note (task 3.3)**: the walk follows the layers as written. At a merged mapping (an include\n  with overrides) only the side or sides holding the next PATH key are followed (both when both write it as\n  mappings, as `deep_merge` merges it). An alias as a `$ref` value counts only for the include's included side,\n  so a key written only in the include's own overrides proceeds.
