A new key below\n    an include root whose `$ref` value is an alias is therefore refused: the placement rules (or `--in-include`)\n    may write it into the included side, which the guard does not decide.\n  - Below PATH, a child that changes as a whole (removed...[truncated 693 chars]
assistant: I fixed Finding 1. The guard now follows only the side of an include that actually holds each key on PATH, so `r.j` and `r.x.p` proceed with and without the flag, and `r.k` and `r.x.q` are still refused. The guard's tests pass (24), and mypy and ruff are clean. I have not run the full suite, as you said you would.\n\n**What changed in `aliases.py`**\n- The walk now follows the nodes as written in each layer (each one a "strand"), not the merged node.\n- **Merged mappings (includes with overrides):** at each step only the side or sides holding the next key are followed, using `deep_merge`'s rule. That is both sides when both write the key as mappings, else the override side when it writes the key, else the included side. Where PATH ends, every side counts.\n- **`$ref: *i`:** the alias counts only on the include's included side. A key written only in the include's own overrides proceeds.\n- **Merge check:** it is now made on the written collection holding the entry, and names that collection's merge-key file (the included file in your case).\n- **Below PATH:** the same per-side rule applies. A changed child that is removed, replaced by another kind or include, or given new keys or items counts on every side. Below that, only changed children are searched.\n- The module docstring is updated to match.\n\n**Tests added**\n- `r.j` (an override key behind `$ref: *i`) proceeds with and without the flag. A new key `r.new` is still refused.\n- `r.x.p` (the included side, beside an override `x: *d`) proceeds. Replacing `r.x` is still refused naming `*d`.\n- The merge-through-both-sides case: `r.m.a` gets the merge refusal naming `inc.yml` and `r.m.<<`, with and without the flag. `r.m.b` and `r.m.c` proceed.\n- The `anchor_uses` test now goes through the real `anchored_location`, checks the written text at each location, and adds `f: &a {$ref: ...}` with `g: *a`. The copied helper is gone.\n- A new test for a replacement that changes a scalar alias, or adds a key to a mapping alias. Without it, two branches had no test that could fail.\n\nThe `r.k` case stays refused through the existing `$ref` test. One caveat on RED: the merge-through-both-sides test passed against the old code too, because that case already worked. Only the two "proceeds" tests failed before the fix.\n\n**Conservative leftovers, recorded in `research.md` under Decision 5 (my only edit to that file)**\n- Where PATH ends, every side counts. So a new key below an include root whose `$ref` value is an alias is still refused. The placement rules, or `--in-include`, may write it into the included side, and the guard does not decide placement.\n- Below PATH, a child that changes as a whole counts on every side. An include written by VALUE counts as changing everything below it.\n- An entry received from a merge key, which `deep_merge` keeps when both sides write the key as mappings, is still refused.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 3.3\n- TASK_BRIEF: alias_sites walks PATH over the written layers (at a merged mapping only the sides holding the next key; every side where PATH ends). The first merged entry gives the 9.2 refusal (with or without the flag). The first alias place gives an AliasSite with expand, else the 5.1/5.2 refusal. An aliased `$ref` counts only for the include's included side. Replacements are searched below PATH only on the changed parts, never below an alias place, outermost first (8.1, 8.2). Nothing found gives () (6.3). anchor_uses indexes anchored written locations. The import-order docstring is updated.\n- FILES_CHANGED: src/troupe/config/writeback/aliases.py (new), src/troupe/config/writeback/__init__.py, tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py (new), spec/spec/config-aliases/research.md (Decision 5 implementation note only)\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 5.1, 5.2, 5.3 (guard before no-op), 6.3, 8.1, 8.2, 9.2, 9.3 (own keys beside `<<` proceed), 4.6 (anchor_uses); requirements term "Reached through an alias"\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: Components and Interfaces → File layer → Guard (aliases.py); System Flows → Guard decision on a walk; Setter and SetCommand (refusal order); Allowed Dependencies; research.md Decision 5; tasks.md Implementation Notes 2.2, 2.3, 3.1, 3.2\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: Round 1: `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py` → "ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'troupe.config.writeback.aliases'" (collection error). Round 2, with the new tests against the round-1 code: "E WriteRefusedError: .../troupe.yml: r.j: value is shared through alias '*i' (anchor '&i'); ..." and "E WriteRefusedError: .../troupe.yml: r.x.p: value is shared through alias '*d' (anchor '&d'); ..." → "FAILED test_an_override_key_behind_an_aliased_ref_proceeds, FAILED test_an_included_key_beside_an_aliased_override_proceeds — 2 failed, 21 passed". The merge-through-both-sides and anchor_uses tests already passed then. Mutation checks after GREEN: side picking forced to both sides → 1 failed; below-PATH search off → 5 failed; via_merge check off → 4 failed; aliased-`$ref` check off → 2 failed; whole-change check off → 1 failed; additions ignored → 1 failed.\n- TESTS_RUN: `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py` → 24 passed; `uv run mypy --strict .` → Success: no issues found in 150 source files; `uv run ruff check .` → All checks passed!; `uv run ruff format --check .` → 151 files already formatted. Full suite not re-run (the coordinator runs it; round 1 gave 3537 passed).\n- CONCERNS: Conservative leftovers recorded in research.md Decision 5: (a) every side counts where PATH ends, so a new key below an include root with `$ref: *i` is refused (placement or --in-include may write the included side); (b) below PATH, a child changing as a whole counts on all sides, and an include in VALUE counts as changing everything below it; (c) a merged entry kept by deep_merge on both-mapping sides is refused. Approved earlier concerns (2), (3) and (6) are unchanged.\n- EVIDENCE: aliases._Guard.run/_step/_sides/_picked/_child_of/_below, _own_alias, _form_site, _descend; alias_refusal/ALIAS_REFUSAL, merge_problem/MERGE_REFUSAL, anchor_uses/anchored_location. Tests: test_an_override_key_behind_an_aliased_ref_proceeds, test_an_included_key_beside_an_aliased_override_proceeds, test_merge_key_on_the_included_side_of_a_merged_mapping, test_an_alias_as_a_ref_value_is_seen_inside_the_include_form (r.k refused), test_replacement_changing_a_scalar_alias_or_adding_to_an_alias_is_refused, test_anchor_uses_index_each_anchored_node_with_its_aliases (real anchored_location, `&a` $ref mapping), plus the round-1 cases.

x: *d\\n  - y\\n\")\n    print(\"C\", run(d3,\"l\",\"[{x: {a: ...[truncated 807 chars]

assistant: ## Review Verdict\n- VERDICT: APPROVED\n- TASK: 3.3 (review round 2)\n- MECHANICAL_RESULTS:\n  - Tests: PASS (`uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py`: 24 passed, exit 0; the full suite is still being run by the implementer, so I have not seen its result)\n  - TBD/TODO grep: CLEAN\n  - Secrets grep: CLEAN\n  - Static checks: PASS (`uv run mypy --strict .` found no issues in 150 files; `uv run ruff check .` passed; `uv run ruff format --check .` reported 151 files already formatted)\n  - Boundary: WITHIN (`writeback/aliases.py`, the import-order docstring in `writeback/__init__.py`, `tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py`, and the Decision 5 decision record in `research.md`)\n  - Boundary audit: CLEAN (the guard is still not wired into the setter, as task 4.1 requires; no new coupling)\n  - RED phase: VERIFIED (the implementer reports that against the round-1 code the new `r.j` and `r.x.p` tests failed with WriteRefusedError, 2 failed and 21 passed)\n- FINDINGS:\n  1. **Round-1 Finding 1 is fixed.** I checked with throwaway probe tests on loaded documents, deleted afterwards:\n     - `r.j` (an override key behind `$ref: *i`) proceeds with and without the flag.\n     - `r.k`, which comes from the included side, is still refused, and with the flag the site is `r.$ref`.\n     - `r.x.p` (the included side next to an override `x: *d`) proceeds.\n     - Replacing `r.x` is still refused naming `*d`.\n     - An alias of an anchored `$ref` mapping with overrides (`b: *a`) refuses both `b.j` and `b.k`, while `a.j` and `a.k` at the anchor proceed. This is correct: `b` is itself an alias place, and the "alias is not the included side's" check handles it.\n     - An alias inside an included file is refused naming `inc.yml`.\n     - A merged entry changed by a replacement is refused.\n     - Replacing a list leaves unchanged alias items alone and refuses changed ones.\n     - The side picked follows the deep-merge rule: both sides only when both write the key as mappings, else the override, else the included side; `$ref` is excluded from the override side. The checks still run in walk order (the node's alias first, then the child's merge entry), outermost first, and the search does not descend below an alias place.\n  2. **Suggestions 2 and 3 are done.**\n     - `test_merge_key_on_the_included_side_of_a_merged_mapping` asserts that `r.m.a` is refused naming `inc.yml` and `r.m.<<`, and that `r.m.b` and `r.m.c` proceed.\n     - `anchor_uses` is now tested through the real `anchored_location`, including the anchored `$ref` mapping case (`f: &a {$ref: ...}`).\n  3. **Ruling on leftover (a): accepted as a recorded conservative choice.**\n     - Req 5.1 makes the deepest existing ancestor the test, not where the new key will be written. The include root `r` gets part of its value through `*i`.\n     - Refusing `r.new` matches what 5.1 literally requires for `r.m.new`. My probe confirms the guard refuses `r.m.new` even though the default placement writes into `troupe.yml`.\n     - The choice is now written down, so the "do not narrow scope quietly" concern is answered.\n     - Suggestion: the stated reason in `research.md` ("the placement rules ... may write it into the included side, which the guard does not decide") is inaccurate. `request.target.placement` already decides where the key goes. My probe shows that in both the default and the `--in-include` case `r.new` is refused, with placement in `troupe.yml` and in `inc.yml` respectively. Reword the reason to cite 5.1's deepest-ancestor rule: the include root's value is partly reached through `*i`.\n  4. **FYI: leftovers (b) and (c) are acceptable.** (b), a change as a whole counting on every side below PATH, is conservative and recorded. (c) follows the existing 2.3 Implementation Note.\n- REMEDIATION: None required. Optionally fix the wording of leftover (a)'s reason in `research.md`, as in finding 3.\n- SUMMARY: The remediation applies "reached through an alias" side by side, as the requirements' definition says; it is tested, including the merge-through-both-sides and anchored-`$ref` cases; the remaining conservative refusals are recorded and acceptable; mechanical checks pass.

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of target resolution and the PatchPlanner in mappings with a merge key (task 3.4).\n\nEach case loads a real theatre configuration with the real loader, resolves the set with the real\n``resolve_target``, plans it with the real ``plan_set`` and compares the patched root file byte for\nbyte. The patched text is read back with the real ``verify_patch`` against the intended\nconfiguration of the plan. An own entry of a mapping with a merge key is changed in place, a new\nkey goes after the mapping's last written entry (which may be the ``<<`` entry), and the ``<<``\nentry and its value are never written. tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat > /tmp/rv/p.py <<'EOF'\nfrom dataclasses import replace\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_set\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.verify import verify_patch\nimport sys\np=Path('/tmp/rv/.troupe/troupe.yml')\nfor text,path,val in [\n ('defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  name: c\\n  model: haiku\\n  <<: *d\\n','coder','{name: c}'),\n ('defaults: &d\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  name: c\\n  model: haiku\\n  <<: *d\\n','coder','{name: c, model: opus}'),\n ('d: &d {}\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *d\\n','coder','{a: 1}'),\n ('d: &d\\n  m: 1\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *d\\n','coder','{m: 1, a: 1}'),\n ('d: &d\\n  m: 1\\ncoder: {<<: *d}\\n','coder','{m: 1, a: 1}'),\n ('d: &d\\n  m: 1\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *d\\n','coder.a','1'),\n]:\n    p.write_text(text); doc=load_config(p); cp=ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    req=resolve_target(doc,cp,parse_value(val,cp))\n    try:\n        plan=plan_set(doc,req)\n        verify_patch(p,doc,plan.patches,replace(req,intended=plan.intended))\n        print(repr(plan.patches[0].new_text))\n    except Exception as e: print(type(e).__name__, e)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/rv/p.py"}

A replacement that drops an own key shadowing a merged key is refused only by read-back verification, with the generic message (4.x may give the specific one). New keys take the style of the last own entry, not of `<<`.\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/writeback/aliases.py src/troupe/config/writeback/planner.py tests/writeback/test_planner_aliases.py spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(config-aliases): write own keys of a mapping that has a merge key\n\nA mapping with `<<` shows a merged view, but only its own entries are written\nin it. The planner now changes own entries in place, adds new keys after the\nlast written entry (which may be `<<`), and deletes own entries around it.\nIt never writes, empties or re-renders the merge key (9.1, 9.3).\n\nAny write that would touch a merged value, including a whole replacement of\nthe mapping, is refused with the guard's message. That way the planner is\nsafe even if a caller skips the guard.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1"}

A slot replacement of an anchored node (for example `set a 2` with `a: &d 1` and `u: *d`, or a kind change such as `set defaults 5` or `'{}'`) is planned as `a: &d 2`. But the intended configuration does not carry the replacement to the alias places, so read-back verification refuses a correct write. Replacements entry by entry (mapping over mapping) already work. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Unit tests of the expander: replace one alias by a copy of its anchored node (config-aliases\ntasks 3.6, 3.7).\n\n``expand_alias`` (``troupe.config.writeback.expand``) replaces the alias of one alias site in its\nfile and returns the new text; it loads nothing. Each case loads a real theatre configuration from\ndisk, takes the alias site from the real guard (``alias_sites`` with ``expand=True`` on a request\nfrom the real TargetResolver), compares the expanded text byte for byte, and loads the expanded\ntext again (as an override of the file) to prove that the expanded configuration is unchanged.\nEvery refusal's message, file and PATH are asserted.\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom support import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.config.errors import ExitCode, WriteRefusedError\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.model import ConfigDocument\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.aliases import AliasSite, alias_sites\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import node_to_plain, plain_equal\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.expand import expand_alias\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\n\n\ndef _config_file(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, text: str) -> Path:\n    return make_theatre(text) / \".troupe\" / \"troupe.yml\"\n\n\ndef _site(document: ConfigDocument, path: str) -> AliasSite:\n    config_path = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    request = resolve_target(document, config_path, parse_value(\"x\", config_path))\n    sites = alias_sites(document, request, expand=True)\n    assert len(sites) == 1\n    return sites[0]\n\n\ndef _expanded(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, text: str, path: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Expand the alias on ``path`` in a theatre whose root file is ``text``; return the new\n    text after proving t...[truncated 13102 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Expander: replace one alias by a copy of its anchored node (config-aliases Requirements 7, 8.3-8.5,\n9.1; research.md Decision 7 and \"Rendering a copy of the anchored node\").\n\n:func:`expand_alias` plans the text edits of one alias site in its file and returns the new text;\nit loads nothing (only the setter reloads). The copy is rendered from the anchored node **as\nwritten** (``AliasRef.anchored``), so styles and line breaks are the file's own.\n\nPreconditions, each refused with :class:`WriteRefusedError` (exit 3) naming the alias's file and\nthe alias site's path:\n\n* the alias is not inside a flow collection (8.4): a block copy cannot be written there;\n* no line from the key's (or item's) indicator to the alias token has a comment (8.5): the line\n  would get a second comment, and a comment between them would be lost;\n* the anchored node's written structure has no mapping with a merge key (9.1): a copy would write\n  ``<<``. Inner alias places and includes are not part of that structure (they are copied as\n  written).\n\nThe key's line (7.1, 7.3, 8.3): the text from the slot's indicator end (after ``:`` or ``-``) to\nthe end of the alias token, plus the spaces after it, is replaced by `` <inline>  <comment>``, or\nby ``  <comment>`` when the copy has no inline part, where the comment is\n:data:`EXPANDED_COMMENT`. The inline part is:\n\n* a single-line scalar's source text, verbatim (keeps its quoting, 7.4), and nothing for an\n  empty null;\n* a multi-line flow scalar rendered by :func:`render_scalar` with its own style preferred (7.4);\n* ``{}``/``[]`` for an empty collection;\n* a literal or folded block scalar's header indicators; its content lines follow the key's line,\n  shifted by the difference between the alias's and the anchor's owner columns (the key or ``-``),\n  so an indentation indicator stays valid. The anchor's header comment is not copied (7.6).\n\nComments inside the anchored node are never copied and no other byte c...[truncated 11311 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport re\np='src/troupe/config/writeback/expand.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold=s[s.index('def _lines_after('):s.index('def _anchored_slot(')]\nnew='''def _lines_after(\n    layout: FileLayout, line: int, lines: tuple[str, ...], line_break: str\n) -> TextEdit:\n    \"\"\"Insert ``lines``, each ending with ``line_break``, after ``line``. At the end of a file\n    without a final break a break comes first: the copied lines end with a break as at the anchor,\n    so a clip or keep block keeps its final line break.\"\"\"\n    copied = \"\".join(f\"{text}{line_break}\" for text in lines)\n    if layout.line_break(line):\n        index = layout.line_start(line + 1)\n        return TextEdit(index, index, copied, EditKind.LINES)\n    index = layout.line_span(line).end\n    return TextEdit(index, index, line_break + copied, EditKind.LINES)\n\n\n'''\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nt='tests/writeback/test_expand.py'\ns=open(t).read()\ns=s.replace('''    \"\"\"Req 7.3, 7.6: at the end of a file without a final line break the content starts on a new\n    line and the file still ends without a break.\"\"\"\n    text = \"a: &d |\\\\n  one\\\\n  two\\\\nb: *d\"\n    expected = \"a: &d |\\\\n  one\\\\n  two\\\\nb: |  # expanded from *d by troupe\\\\n  one\\\\n  two\"''','''    \"\"\"Req 7.3, 7.4: at the end of a file without a final line break the content starts on a new\n    line, and its last line ends with a break as at the anchor, so the block keeps its value.\"\"\"\n    text = \"a: &d |\\\\n  one\\\\n  two\\\\nb: *d\"\n    expected = \"a: &d |\\\\n  one\\\\n  two\\\\nb: |  # expanded from *d by troupe\\\\n  one\\\\n  two\\\\n\"''')\nopen(t,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngrep -n \"at the anchor, so the block\" tests/writeback/test_expand.py; uv run ruff format src tests/writeback/test_expand.py; uv run pytest tests/writeback/test_expand.py -q 2>&1 | tail -3"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; mkdir -p /tmp/exp/.troupe; cat > /tmp/exp_probe.py <<'EOF'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.aliases import alias_sites\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.compare import node_to_plain, plain_equal\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.expand import expand_alias\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.errors import TroupeError\ncases = [\n  (\"x: [&d a, b]\\ny: *d\\n\", \"y\", {}),\n  (\"x: {k: &d 'q'}\\ny:\\n  - z: *d\\n\", \"y.0.z\", {}),\n  (\"i: &i file:p.yml\\nj:\\n  $ref: *i\\n  k: 1\\n\", \"j.m\", {\"p.yml\": \"m: 1\\n\"}),\n  (\"j:\\n  $ref: file:p.yml\\n  m: &d 5\\nk: *d\\n\", \"k\", {\"p.yml\": \"m: 1\\n\"}),\n  (\"a: &d |\\n  x\\n# c\\nb: *d\\n\", \"b\", {}),\n  (\"a: &d |-\\n  x\\nb:\\n- *d\\n\", \"b.0\", {}),\n]\nfor text, path, files in cases:\n    root = Path(\"/tmp/exp/.troupe\"); \n    for f in root.iterdir(): f.unlink()\n    (root/\"troupe.yml\").write_text(text)\n    for n,t in files.items(): (root/n).write_text(t)\n    doc = load_config(root/\"troupe.yml\")\n    p = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    try:\n        req = resolve_target(doc, p, parse_value(\"x\", p))\n        site = alias_sites(doc, req, expand=True)[0]\n        out = expand_alias(doc, site)\n        new = list(out.values())[0]\n        re = load_config(root/\"troupe.yml\", out)\n        print(repr(new), plain_equal(node_to_plain(re.root), node_to_plain(doc.root), ordered=True))\n    except TroupeError as e:\n        print(\"ERR\", e)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/exp_probe.py"}

tool: SendMessage {"content": "Task 3.7 review round 1: REJECTED, with one Impor…", "message": "Task 3.7 review round 1: REJECTED, with one Important finding. Fix it and report again with the full `## Status Report` block, including RED_PHASE_OUTPUT for the new tests.\n\nFinding 1 (Important; your concern 1): a collection copy at the end of a file that has no final line break makes the file end with one. Examples: `d: &d\\n  a: 1\\nx: *d` becomes `...x:  # expanded from *d by troupe\\n  a: 1\\n`, and `l:\\n  - *d` with no final break does the same. This breaks config-set-writeback Req 11.6: lines added after the last line of a file without a final break must leave the file still ending without one. It also breaks product principle 1 and Req 7.6. The only valid reason for a final break is a block scalar with clip or keep chomping, which needs it or its value changes.\n\nRemediation:\n- At the end of a file without a final break, write the leading break and then the copied lines joined by breaks, with no break after the last line.\n- Keep a trailing break only when the copy's last line ends the content of a clip or keep block scalar (the 3.6 case, still pinned by `test_block_scalar_copy_at_the_end_of_a_file_without_a_final_break`). Resolved (round 1, Finding 1): at the end of a file without a final line break, the copy no longer adds a final break, as config-set-writeback 11.6 and product principle 1 require. It still adds one when the last copied line ends a clip or keep block scalar, which needs it to keep its value. Each copy records whether its last line needs a final break (`final_break`), and `_lines_after` writes the closing break only when it is set. I probed the following, all byte for byte and all reloading equal:\n     - a mapping copy and a `- *d` copy at the end of the file, in LF and CRLF files: no final break;\n     - copies ending in a clip, keep or nested folded block scalar: final break kept;\n     - copies ending in a strip block scalar, `|2-`, or an empty clip block (`a: |` with no content): no final break;\n     - a block scalar that is not the last entry: no final break;\n     - the top-level 3.6 scalar path: clip keeps the break, strip adds none;\n     - the same copy in a file that has a final break: unchanged.\n  2. Resolved (round 1, Finding 2): `test_key_written_over_several_lines_is_rendered_in_its_style` now pins the multi-line key fallback byte for byte.\n  3. Covered: the new parametrized tests (`[mapping|list-item|strip-block-scalar]` and `[clip|keep]`) compare exact bytes and check reload equality. They would fail if the change were reverted, and the round-2 RED run shows that.\n  4. FYI (unchanged from round 1, existing layout code): the file's indentation step is measured from the first nested mapping. When that mapping starts with an explicit `?` key, the layout measures the key's column after the `?`. The new key test avoids this by putting an ordinary mapping first, and its docstring says so. This is not part of 3.7.\n- REMEDIATION: none\n- SUMMARY: The fix for the missing final line break is correct and tested, so the collection copies now match how the file is written in every shape I probed and meet Req 7.2, 7.4, 7.5, 7.6 and 8.3.

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; mkdir -p /tmp/r38 && cat > /tmp/r38/run.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys, tempfile\nfrom dataclasses import replace\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom troupe.config.loader import load_config\nfrom troupe.config.paths import ConfigPath\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.planner import plan_set\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.target import resolve_target\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.value import parse_value\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.verify import verify_patch\ncases = [\n (\"a: &d 1\\nu: *d\\n\",\"a\",\"2\"),\n (\"a: &d 1  # c\\nu: *d\\n\",\"a\",\"2\"),\n (\"a: &d 1  # c\\nu: *d\\n\",\"a\",\"{k: 1}\"),\n (\"a: &d 1\\nu: *d\\n\",\"a\",\"{}\"),\n (\"shared:\\n  defaults: &d  # anchored\\n    model: opus\\ncoder: *d\\n\",\"shared.defaults\",\"5\"),\n (\"shared:\\n  defaults: &d  # anchored\\n    model: opus\\ncoder: *d\\n\",\"shared.defaults\",\"{}\"),\n (\"defaults: &d  # anchored\\n  model: opus\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *d\\n  t: 1\\n\",\"defaults\",\"{}\"),\n (\"defaults: &d {model: opus}  # anchored\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *d\\n  t: 1\\n\",\"defaults\",\"{}\"),\n (\"defaults: &d {model: opus}\\ncoder:\\n  <<: *d\\n\",\"defaults\",\"5\"),\n (\"l: &d [1]\\nu: *d\\n\",\"l\",\"[]\"),\n (\"l: &d\\n  - 1\\nu: *d\\n\",\"l\",\"x\"),\n (\"l:\\n  - &d 1  # c\\n  - *d\\n\",\"l.0\",\"2\"),\n]\nfor text, path, value in cases:\n    d = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()); (d/\"troupe.yml\").write_text(text)\n    doc = load_config(d/\"troupe.yml\"); p = ConfigPath.parse(path)\n    req = resolve_target(doc, p, parse_value(value, p))\n    try:\n        plan = plan_set(doc, req)\n        print(repr(plan.patches[0].new_text)); print(\"  intended\", plan.intended)\n        verify_patch(d/\"troupe.yml\", doc, plan.patches, replace(req, intended=plan.intended))\n        print(\"  OK\")\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(\"  ERR\", type(e).__name__, e)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/r38/run.py"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"- [ ] 3.8 Replicate a replacement\",\"- [x] 3.8 Replicate a replacement\",1)\na=\"Alias places needed no rebuild change, because they share the anchored node's written locations.\"\nassert a in s\ns=s.replace(a,\"Alias places needed no rebuild change for changes *inside* an anchored node, because they share its written locations (a replacement of the anchored node itself needed 3.8).\")\nb=\"- [ ] 4. Integration: the `set` command\"\nnew='''- [ ] 3.9 Keep an anchor on a block list item that is replaced by a mapping\n  - Added during implementation (review of 3.8). Replacing an anchored block list item with a mapping (`l:\\\\n  - &d 1\\\\n`, then `set l.0 '{k: 1}'`) writes `- &d k: 1`. YAML reads that anchor as belonging to the key `k`, so read-back refuses the set (exit 3, nothing written). Requirement 4.3 expects the set to succeed with `&d` kept on the new value. The mapping therefore starts on the line after `- &d`, at the list's item content indentation, under config-set-writeback's rules for a list item's scalar replaced by a block mapping. A list item replaced by a block list is handled the same way when needed.\n  - Done when byte-exact planner tests on real files show `- &d` followed by the mapping on the next lines for a block list item (with and without an alias of it elsewhere, and with an end-of-line comment after the anchor), each reading back with the anchor kept and every alias seeing the new value. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``troupe config set`` refusals for aliases, merge keys and anchors, end to end (config-aliases).\n\nEvery refusal runs the real ``troupe`` console script on a real theatre and checks the whole\ncontract of a refusal: exit 3, empty stdout, one stderr line naming the file, PATH and the alias\nor anchor (and ``--expand-alias`` for a set reached through an alias, Requirement 5), every file\nunder the theatre unchanged byte for byte, and no history event beyond the import that the first\ncommand ran (Requirements 4.6, 4.7, 5.1-5.4, 9.2).\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom set_support import assert_file_bytes, run_config, run_set, snapshot_tree\nfrom support import CliResult, MakeTheatre\n\nISSUE_2 = (\n    \"defaults: &d\\n\"\n    \"  model: opus\\n\"\n    \"coder:\\n\"\n    \"  settings: *d\\n\"\n    \"reviewer:\\n\"\n    \"  settings: *d\\n\"\n)\n\"\"\"The reporter's file of tracker issue #2.\"\"\"\n\nALIAS_PROBLEM = (\n    \"value is shared through alias '*{name}' (anchor '&{name}'); setting it here would change \"\n    \"every place that uses the anchor; use --expand-alias to give this place its own copy\"\n)\n\nMERGE_PROBLEM = (\n    \"value comes from merge key '<<' at {key_path}; merge keys are never written, \"\n    \"and --expand-alias cannot expand them\"\n)\n\nROOT_FILE = \".troupe/troupe.yml\"\n\n\ndef _history(theatre: Path) -> bytes:\n    result = run_config(\"history\", \"--theatre\", str(theatre), cwd=theatre)\n    assert (result.exit_code, result.stderr) == (0, \"\"), result.stderr\n    return result.stdout\n\n\ndef _assert_refused(result: CliResult, file: Path, path: str, problem: str) -> None:\n    assert (result.exit_code, result.stdout) == (3, b\"\"), result.stderr\n    assert result.stderr == f\"{file}: {path}: {problem}\\n\", result.stderr\n\n\ndef _refused_without_change(\n    theatre: Path, path: str, value: ...[truncated 5659 chars]
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''    [(\"m.model\", \"x\"), (\"m.temperature\", \"0.3\"), (\"m\", \"{own: 1}\")],\n    ids=[\"merged-value\", \"new-key-in-a-merged-mapping\", \"replacement-dropping-merged-values\"],''','''    [(\"m.model\", \"x\"), (\"m.model\", \"opus\"), (\"m\", \"{own: 1}\")],\n    ids=[\"merged-value\", \"no-op-at-a-merged-value\", \"replacement-dropping-merged-values\"],''')\ns=s.replace('''    problem = MERGE_PROBLEM.format(key_path=\"m.<<\")\n    if path == \"m.temperature\":\n        # The deepest existing ancestor of PATH is the merged value itself.\n        problem = MERGE_PROBLEM.format(key_path=\"m.<<\")\n    _refused_without_change(theatre, path, value, ROOT_FILE, problem)''','''    _refused_without_change(theatre, path, value, ROOT_FILE, MERGE_PROBLEM.format(key_path=\"m.<<\"))''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py 2>&1 | tail -30"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nstart=s.index('Temporary (config-aliases tasks.md')\nend=s.index('\"\"\"\\n', start)\ns=s[:start]+'''Since config-aliases (step 6) the guard runs right after target resolution and before the no-op\ncheck: a set reached through an alias or a merge key is refused (exit 3) even when it would change\nnothing, and every planner refusal comes after it (config-aliases 5.1-5.3, 9.2; research.md\nDecision 5).\n'''+s[end:]\ns=s.replace('''    document = theatre.document\n    _refuse_alias_constructs(document, path)  # temporary: task 4.1 removes this refusal\n    request = resolve_target(document, path, value, in_include=in_include)\n''','''    document = theatre.document\n    request = resolve_target(document, path, value, in_include=in_include)\n    # Before the no-op check, so a no-op through an alias is refused too (config-aliases 5.3).\n    alias_sites(document, request, expand=False)\n''')\na=s.index('def _refuse_alias_constructs')\nb=s.index('def write_patches')\ns=s[:a]+s[b:]\ns=s.replace('from collections.abc import Iterator, Sequence','from collections.abc import Sequence')\ns=s.replace('''from troupe.config.model import (\n    ConfigDocument,\n    ConfigMapping,\n    ConfigNode,\n    ConfigSequence,\n    SourceFile,\n)''','from troupe.config.model import SourceFile')\ns=s.replace('from troupe.config.writeback.atomic','from troupe.config.writeback.aliases import alias_sites\\nfrom troupe.config.writeback.atomic')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nsed -n 1,80p src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py; uv run pytest -q tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py 2>&1 | tail -15"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; cat >> tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py <<'EOF'\n\n\ndef test_replacement_by_another_collection_kind_compares_children_by_kind(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Req 5.2, 6.3: a mapping replaced by a list (or a list by a mapping) has no intended child\n    for any of its keys; without an alias below PATH the guard proceeds, and with one it refuses\n    naming the alias.\"\"\"\n    document = _document(make_theatre, \"m:\\n  model: x\\nl:\\n  - 1\\n\")\n    for path, value in ((\"m\", \"[a]\"), (\"l\", \"{a: 1}\")):\n        for expand in (False, True):\n            assert _sites(document, path, value, expand=expand) == ()\n    document = _document(make_theatre, SHARED, files=None) if False else _document(\n        make_theatre, \"d: &d\\n  model: x\\nc:\\n  s: *d\\n\", files=None\n    )\n    error = _refusal(document, \"c\", \"[a]\", expand=False)\n    assert (error.path, error.problem) == (\"c\", _alias_problem(\"d\"))\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py -k by_another 2>&1 | grep -E \"^E .*Error|passed|failed\" | head"}

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/config/writeback/aliases.py'\ns=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace('''        for key, child in _children(node):\n            child_intended = _child(intended, key)\n''','''        same_kind = _same_kind(node, intended)\n        for key, child in _children(node):\n            child_intended = _child(intended, key) if same_kind else _MISSING\n''')\ns=s.replace('''def _children(node: ConfigNode)''','''def _same_kind(node: ConfigNode, intended: _Intended) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Whether ``intended`` is a collection of ``node``'s kind, so that its children are found by\n    ``node``'s keys (a mapping replaced by a list, or the reverse, has none of them).\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(intended, IncludeDirective):\n        intended = _MISSING if intended.content is None else intended.content\n    if isinstance(node, ConfigMapping):\n        return isinstance(intended, dict)\n    return isinstance(node, ConfigSequence) and isinstance(intended, list)\n\n\ndef _children(node: ConfigNode)''')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py tests/test_set_overrides.py tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py 2>&1 | tail -3"}

Task 4.4 must add the e2e case for 9.2 with `--expand-alias`, still refused.\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/writeback/aliases.py src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py tests/writeback/test_alias_guard.py spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(config-aliases): run the guard in set and refuse writes through aliases and merge keys\n\nThis is the fix for issue #2. `set coder.settings.model sonnet` used to be\nable to change `defaults` and every other agent through `*d`. whole result:\nexit 0 with empty stdout and stderr, the changed file byte for byte (only the requested change, the\n``&name`` property and every ``*name`` alias and ``<<`` entry kept as written), every other file of\nthe theatre unchanged, ``get`` at the anchor and at each place that uses it, and the history events\nthe set recorded: one ``set`` or ``remove`` event per changed leaf path, at the anchor and at every\nalias place and merge use, all in one change with cli lineage (Requirements 4.1-4.5, 9.1, 9.3,\n10.1, 10.2).\n\nTraceability: every test names the requirement IDs it proves at the start of its docstring.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any, Final\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom set_support import assert_file_bytes, run_config, run_set, snapshot_tree\nfrom support import MakeTheatre\n\nACTOR: Final = {\"kind\": \"agent\", \"id\": \"writer\"}\n\"\"\"The actor every set in this file runs as (``TROUPE_ACTOR=agent:writer``).\"\"\"\n\nOTHER_FILES: Final = {\"notes/readme.md\": \"# not configuration\\n\", \".troupe/spare.yml\": \"x: 1\\n\"}\n\"\"\"Files of the theatre that no set here may touch.\"\"\"\n\nEvent = tuple[str, str, Any, Any]\n\"\"\"One recorded event as ``(op, path, old, new)``.\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass AnchorWrite:\n    \"\"\"One set at an anchor: the file before, the set, the file after, reads and events.\"\"\"\n\n    before: str\n    path: str\n    value: str\n    after: str\n    events: list[Event]\n    reads: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    \"\"\"``get PATH`` -> its exact stdout after the set.\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _history(theatre: Path) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    result = run_config(\"history\", You said you would run the full suite, so I did not re-run it.\n\nThe suggested mutation (disabling the merge-key re-merge in `_Rebuild`) does not make the new test fail. The merge use's `tools` entry is the anchored list itself, so the rebuild picks up the append whether or not the re-merge runs. I used a different mutation that hits the merge-use path: the new test fails under it.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 4.2\n- TASK_BRIEF: Byte-exact e2e tests through the real `troupe config set` CLI.\n  - A set at an anchor writes at the anchor, keeps `&name`, and leaves every `*name` and `<<` line unchanged.\n  - New keys and list appends inside an anchored node are seen at each alias and through `<<: *d`, but not where an own key shadows them.\n  - A replaced anchored node keeps `&d`.\n  - A replacement that contains an anchored node changes it in place.\n  - Own-key writes in a mapping with `<<` leave `<<` unchanged.\n  - Each write records one `set`/`remove` event per changed leaf path at the anchor, at every alias place and at every merge use, in one change with cli lineage. No other file changes.\n- FILES_CHANGED: tests/test_set_anchors.py (new)\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 4.1, 4.2, 4.3, 4.4, 4.5, 9.1, 9.3, 10.1, 10.2 (choices 7, 8 and 10 of the Aliases contract)\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: Testing Strategy, E2E "Anchor writes" (the 4.6 and 4.7 refusals are covered by tests/test_set_alias_refusals.py from task 4.1), and "Refusals" 9.3\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: All cases passed the first time, since this is a validation task, so I showed failability with deliberate mutations. All were restored and `git status` shows only the new test file.\n  - Round 1:\n    - Turning off the anchored replacement record in `shared.py` and the anchor-property check in `layout.py` together gave 5 failed, 10 passed. Each failure was `assert (3, b'', '/tm...figuration\n') == (0, b'', '')`, meaning read-back refused the set.\n    - The `layout.py` change alone gave 1 failed, on `[list-item-scalar-by-mapping]`.\n  - Round 2:\n    - The suggested mutation (`if node.merge is not None:` changed to `if False:` in `_Rebuild.plain`) gave 2 failed (`[mapping-by-mapping]` and `test_new_key_...merge_use`). The new list test passed. The intended configuration comes only from the expanded document, and nothing checks that the expansion kept the original configuration's values. YAML allows an anchor name to be redefined, and an alias refers to the most recent definition before it (choice 15). So an inner alias copied into the copy can bind to a different anchor. The reviewer reproduced two cases:\n- Change: `e: &e\\n  m: 1\\nd: &d\\n  x: *e\\nf: &e\\n  m: 9\\na: *d\\n` with `set_value(..., a.y, 5, expand_alias=True)` writes `a.x: *e` into the copy. `a.x.m` silently changes from 1 to 9, and `SetChange(a.x.m, 1, 9)` is recorded.\n- No-op: add `k: 1` to `d` and run `set a.k 1` with expansion. If they differ, raise WriteRefusedError (exit 3) naming the alias's file, the user's PATH and the alias. This must happen before any write.\n- Add tests in tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py for the redefined-anchor case, both as a change and as a no-op. I ran `tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py` (14 passed), `tests/writeback` with the `tests/test_set_*.py` files (1770 passed), mypy and ruff, all clean; I have not re-run the full suite after this change. Nothing is committed and `tasks.md` is unchanged.\n\n**The fix (`src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py`, in `_expanded`)**\n- **Value check:** after each reload, the re-resolved `request.current` is compared, in order, with the original configuration. If they differ, the set is refused with `WriteRefusedError` (exit 3), naming the expanded alias's file and the user's PATH. The problem is the new constant `EXPANSION_CHANGES_VALUES`: "expanding alias '*d' would change values elsewhere (an alias in its copy would refer to an anchor redefined before this place)". The message names the alias being expanded (`*d`), not the inner `*e` in your suggested wording, because the setter does not know which inner alias rebound.\n- **Stuck loop:** the plain `RuntimeError` is now a `WriteRefusedError` (exit 3) naming the alias's file and PATH, with the problem in the constant `EXPANSION_STUCK`: "alias '*d' is still an alias after its expansion".\n- Both refusals are built by one small helper, `_alias_refusal`.\n\n**Tests (`tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py`)**\n- `test_expansion_that_would_change_values_elsewhere_is_refused`, for the reviewer's file with `k: 1` added to `d`, run twice:\n  - as a change, `a.y 5`;\n  - as a no-op, `a.k 1`.\n- `test_an_expansion_that_makes_no_progress_stops_the_loop` now expects the `WriteRefusedError` and the unchanged history.\n- Each of these asserts the exit code, exact problem, file, PATH, unchanged tree snapshot and unchanged history.\n\n## Status Report\n- STATUS: READY_FOR_REVIEW\n- TASK: 4.3\n- TASK_BRIEF: With expand_alias=True, set_value expands one alias per round in memory (outermost first), reloads, re-resolves and re-runs the guard until no site remains. Each round must keep the configuration unchanged, else exit 3 naming the alias's file and PATH (redefined-anchor rebinding). Then it runs the usual no-op check and plan on the expanded document, verifies the original document with final patches on the original SourceFiles, diffs original against after (no event for the expansion), and writes every changed file once atomically with restore on failure. Expander and stuck-loop refusals are exit 3 with the user's PATH.\n- FILES_CHANGED: src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py, tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py\n- REQUIREMENTS_CHECKED: 6.3, 7.5, 7.7, 8.1, 8.2, 8.4, 8.5, 8.6, 9.2, 10.1, 10.2, 10.3, 10.4\n- DESIGN_CHECKED: Setter and SetCommand; System Flows "set with the guard and the expansion loop"; Expander preconditions; research.md Decision 6\n- RED_PHASE_OUTPUT: Round 1: `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py` -> 12 failed (TypeError: set_value() got an unexpected keyword argument 'expand_alias'). Round 2 (new and changed tests, before the fix): `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py` -> 3 failed, 11 passed: test_an_expansion_that_makes_no_progress_stops_the_loop (`RuntimeError: alias at coder.settings is still an alias after its expansion`), test_expansion_that_would_change_values_elsewhere_is_refused[a.y-5] and [a.k-1] (`Failed: DID NOT RAISE WriteRefusedError`)\n- TESTS_RUN: `uv run pytest -q tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py` -> 14 passed; `uv run pytest -q -n auto tests/writeback tests/test_set_*.py` -> 1770 passed; `uv run mypy --strict .` -> Success: no issues found in 155 source files; `uv run ruff check .` -> All checks passed!; `uv run ruff format --check .` -> 156 files already formatted. The full suite was run before this round (3691 passed) and is left to the coordinator now.\n- CONCERNS: (1) The redefined-anchor refusal names the expanded alias (`*d`), since the setter cannot tell which inner alias rebound. The full `uv run pytest -n auto -q` gave 3693 passed. Both exited 0.\n  - TBD/TODO grep: CLEAN\n  - Secrets grep: CLEAN\n  - Static checks: PASS. `mypy --strict .`, `ruff check .` and `ruff format --check .` are all clean.\n  - Boundary: WITHIN. Only src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py and tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py changed.\n  - Boundary audit: CLEAN. Only setter calls `load_config`. The expander is used only through `expand_alias`.\n  - RED phase: VERIFIED. Round 1 failed with TypeError on the missing `expand_alias` keyword. Round 2 reports 3 failures: two "DID NOT RAISE" and one RuntimeError. These match the new refusal tests and the changed stuck-loop test.\n- FINDINGS:\n  1. **Round 1 Critical Finding 1 is fixed.** After each reload, `_expanded` compares the re-resolved `request.current` with the original whole configuration, in order. A difference raises a `WriteRefusedError` (exit 3) before any write. The error names the expanded alias's file (`alias.token.file.path`) and the user's PATH. Both reproductions are now covered by `test_expansion_that_would_change_values_elsewhere_is_refused`: the change `a.y 5` and the no-op `a.k 1`. Each asserts exit code, problem, file, PATH, unchanged tree and unchanged history. This satisfies 7.5, 8.6, 10.2 and 10.4.\n  2. **The comparison does not reject legitimate expansions.** I ran 8 extra cases on scratch theatres (since deleted), all through `set_value(..., expand_alias=True)`. Every one succeeded, and only the leaves at PATH changed. The cases were:\n     - a mapping holding a literal block scalar, a quoted key and value, an include string, a `$ref` include with overrides, an empty null, and a nested flow mapping;\n     - a CRLF file;\n     - a block list item alias (`- *d`);\n     - an anchored flow list;\n     - an indentation step of 4;\n     - an alias inside an included file;\n     - nested aliases with two inner `*e`;\n     - a no-op with an inner scalar alias.\n\n     The comparison is sound because `request.current` is the whole plain configuration with include directives kept, and the copy writes the same keys in the same order with the same directives. Mappings with merge keys are refused before this check.\n  3. **FYI, concern (2) is resolved.** The stuck-loop guard now raises a `WriteRefusedError` (`EXPANSION_STUCK`, exit 3) naming the file and PATH. The test asserts this and also checks that nothing is written and nothing is recorded.\n  4. **FYI, unchanged from round 1.** The merge-key message that names `c.s.<<` is accepted 3.6 behaviour, and the user's PATH is carried. For a no-op with expansion, `written=(file,)` with `changes=()` is correct. On a write failure the original bytes are restored, and the other alias still reads the anchor after read-back.\n  5. **Suggestion (non-blocking).** `EXPANSION_CHANGES_VALUES` names the outer expanded alias, not the inner alias that rebound. This is acceptable because the setter cannot identify that alias. A later expander-level refusal in 3.6/4.4 could name `*e` exactly. It is worth an Implementation Notes entry for 4.4's e2e coverage.\n- REMEDIATION: None required.\n- SUMMARY: The remediation adds a whole-configuration check after each expansion round, so an expansion that would change values off PATH is refused before any write; legitimate expansions in every case I probed still succeed, and all tests and static checks pass.

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo; python3 - <<'EOF'\np='spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md'; s=open(p).read()\ns=s.replace(\"- [ ] 4.3 Expand before setting\",\"- [x] 4.3 Expand before setting\",1)\ns+=\"- 4.3: after each expansion round, the setter checks that the whole configuration is unchanged. If it changed, it refuses with exit 3 (`EXPANSION_CHANGES_VALUES`, naming the expanded alias). This is the safety net for a copied inner alias that binds to a redefined anchor of the same name. A stuck expansion is refused with exit 3 too (`EXPANSION_STUCK`). Expander refusals are re-raised with the user's PATH. The expander module is imported as `expander`. Task 4.4 should cover the redefined-anchor refusal end to end.\\n\"\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit add src/troupe/config/writeback/setter.py tests/writeback/test_setter_aliases.py spec/spec/config-aliases/tasks.md\ngit commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(config-aliases): expand aliases before setting in the set use case\n\nWith expansion requested, the set use case expands one alias at a time in\nmemory, reloads and re-checks until no alias on PATH remains. It then runs the\nusual set on the expanded configuration, so \"sets the value in the copy\" uses\nevery earlier write rule. Verification, events and restore all compare with\nthe original files, so expansion records no event and a failed write puts the\noriginal bytes back.\n\nAfter each round the setter also checks that the whole configuration is\nunchanged. The review found that YAML anchor redefinition can make a copied\ninner alias bind to another anchor and silently change values elsewhere.\nRefusing that case is the conservative choice.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1"}
